# AnimeOS - AI-Native Animation Production Platform

> **DSH decides · Tools execute · Engine builds · State remembers**

AnimeOS is an AI-native production studio for animated and illustrated storytelling. It natively supports **donghua**, **anime**, **manhua/manhwa (comics)** and adjacent formats through one unified, stateful production pipeline - designed so an AI director (DSH) drives the studio through tools, not ad-hoc chat.

## Core thesis

```
DSH decides → tools execute → engine builds → render → DSH evaluates → revise → approve
```

Every step writes to a persistent production universe: projects, seasons, episodes, scenes, shots, characters (with episode-resolved states), relationships, assets, terminology and continuity events. Nothing lives only in a chat log.

## Feature map

| Area | What it does |
|------|--------------|
| **DSH Director** | 88-tool production API behind a 4-round INTENT → PLAN → EXECUTE → OBSERVE orchestrator. Mid-turn project switching, full execution trace rendered in the console. The director authors **dialogue** (`set_shot_dialogue`), **panel art** (`generate_panel_art`), **character model sheets** (`generate_model_sheet`), the **art style direction** (`set_art_style`), **scene staffing** (`auto_assign_scene_team` - specialism-aware artist routing + content-matched LoRA attachment, load-balanced), **voice direction** (`direct_voice_takes` - state-aware delivery for every VOICE cue) and **voice casting** (`cast_voice_actor` - roster artists speak for characters) itself, pins **state arcs** across a shot range or across whole scenes of an episode (`set_state_arc`, `scope:'episode'`), paints **reusable arc templates** over a range in one call (`apply_arc_template` - the built-ins plus the production's saved shapes AND the studio library), **suggests a template** when the creator describes a beat in prose (`suggest_arc_template` ranks the registry against their words and hands back the apply framing), steers **per-state pose presets** (`set_state_poses` - a development state's body language, auto-resolved from the label on `create_character_state`), and proposes the webtoon export pre-flight in the same turn a diff detects staleness. **Ensemble beats**: pass `characters:[{name, stateLabel?}]` and one template lands on several speakers in ONE batch (per-speaker state overrides, unresolvable speakers skipped and reported, one combined Direction impact). The arc tools report the **direction impact** they just caused (which takes went stale) so DSH offers the re-render in the same turn, attach **playable arc chips** to the trace (the reply itself streams the arc's stored takes in story order), render **same-turn auditions** (one proposed read per engaged speaker of the exact stamped line, A/B against the stored take) and record **per-scope usage counts** so the registry shows which shapes each show actually applies. **Cadence scheduler**: `create_schedule` arms recurring studio work (a PLAN_RUN walks an approved plan a few steps per fire, the nightly-breakdown pattern; a REPAINT_QUEUE watch ticks render jobs and starts a supervised re-paint pass when the universe queue is dirty) fired by an in-process minute loop between conversations, every fire landing as a production event the creator audits from the scheduler panel (`steer_schedule` runs/enables/disables/deletes). **Art-aware continuity**: `check_art_continuity` scans for panel art that predates the state or anchor it should carry and can run a vision comparison of a hero panel against the character's canonical model sheet, filing ART_DRIFT / ART_VERIFIED events automatically. **Universe-facts vision checks**: `add_universe_fact` registers the world's canon rules (glowing blade spirits, two moons over the arena, the mask that never comes off) and `check_universe_facts` runs a vision model over a hero panel against every active fact - a holds/confidence verdict per fact lands as FACT_HELD / FACT_BROKEN events, and confident violations rank into the re-render queue in the Continuity view for a one-click panel re-render and re-check. **Per-episode plan templates**: `land_episode_plan` instantiates a shaped plan (beat-breakdown, panel-pass, render-pass, canon-audit) for one episode with its real scene/shot numbers resolved at landing time, PROPOSED for the creator's review gate - and any **creator-authored variation** saved in the plans panel (cloned from a built-in, reshaped row by row, `{episode}`/`{latestScene}`/`{title}` tokens resolving at landing, tools validated against the registry on save, PROJECT or STUDIO scope, usage-counted) lands by name or id the same way. **Identity scoring**: `score_panel_identity` vision-scores a panel (or, source:'render', a frame of the shot's FINISHED CLIP) against EVERY featured character's model sheet (0..1 per character plus per-aspect scores), targeted, worst-first batch, or defaulting to the worst artifact; the bar is the SOURCE's bar - storyboard panels repaint below 60%, but the SHIPPING PIXELS answer at 70% (THE BAR IS MEASURED: `measure_identity_bar` walks the finished renders, vision-scores each against the same sheets, and reads the honest distribution - cleared / average / worst / the repaint queue), `cast_identity_pass` reads the standing BY NAME (THE CAST ANSWERS THE BAR: every member's real per-character readings roll into one standing - CLEARING, BELOW with the worst shot ref named (the re-render queue by name), UNTESTED (anchored, never measured), UNANCHORED (no sheet - nothing to answer to yet)), a worst below the source's bar earns the re-paint (panel) or re-render (shipping pixels) offer in the result itself, and `reanchor_character` RE-ANCHORS a character whose drift curve declines because the design itself moved - the canonical sheet regenerates from the current design text, an IDENTITY_REANCHOR event restarts the curve's baseline, and recent panels re-score against the new sheet. **The studio writes to the creator**: a DAILY_DIGEST schedule posts a digest of the last 24 hours (renders, plan steps, schedule fires, canon and identity-drift headlines, queue pressure) as a production event the digest panel shows, and `post_digest` lands one on demand; `create_schedule` accepts `webhookUrl` / `digestEmail` so the digest DELIVERS itself beyond the studio (webhook POST, SMTP email via `ANIMEOS_SMTP_URL`), with every delivery outcome recorded on the digest and targets steerable any time; and since iteration 71 the push is PER-MEMBER (the My outbound panel subscribes each member's own webhook/email channels; digest posts and release slates fan out to the subscribed crew + OWNERs; every attempt lands on the NotificationDelivery ledger - SENT, REFUSED or FAILED with the detail). **And it ships**: `publish_cut` stages a platform publish package on the delivery spine (YOUTUBE, BILIBILI, DOUYIN, TIKTOK, STUDIO_INGEST) - the episode's latest cut is probed and conformance-checked against the preset (a 16:9 cut honestly FAILS a 9:16 platform), the package carries clamped metadata, a provenance credits line and an SRT built from the episode's dialogue, and the integration status is honest (credentials present, or a manual hand-off) - no network upload ever happens silently; `upload_package` then runs the platform's REAL upload adapter when the creator's credentials are set (YOUTUBE's resumable init+PUT, TIKTOK/DOUYIN's init-then-put, BILIBILI's multipart submit), with every outcome - OK or the honest FAILED naming the missing env key - appended to the package's PUBLISH event. **Voice cloning**: `train_voice_clone` trains a character's own voice from their rendered takes through the configured cloning provider (`ANIMEOS_VOICE_CLONE_URL`) and persists the trained voice id on the character - their lines then perform with THEIR voice wherever the provider can render it (state voice variants still deliberately override; the catalog voice stays the honest fallback). **Localization with memory**: `translate_subtitles` translates an episode's dialogue (or a pasted SRT) into any language with the Terminology memory ENFORCED - canonical renderings ride every batch prompt and a deterministic post-pass re-writes any cue the model drifted from, unknown-but-consistent renderings come back as suggestions to adopt (`create_terminology` grows the memory), and the translated SRT lands in `public/subtitles/` as a TRANSLATION event. **Native Blender designer**: `blender_asset_build` designs a character or environment as a versioned .blend LIBRARY ASSET from the production's design text, `blender_asset_inspect` vision-scores the asset preview against the canonical sheet, `blender_exec` runs raw bpy refinement passes in the studio's own runtime, `blender_viewport_shot` refreshes previews, `blender_asset_library` reads what exists - READY assets ride every matching render payload (design once, render many), and the whole DESIGN FACILITY stands behind it - named design laws (materials, lighting, motion, variation, sculpt, fx, physics), a self-correcting design loop (design_audit -> design_fix -> re-audit), learned craft memory (learned retopo flows, vision-guided sculpt plans, and LEARNED MOTION FLOWS - `learn_motion_flow` adopts a shot's VERIFIED keyed performance as the production's named memory of how a pose transition is performed for a dramatic register, and `set_shot_choreography` re-performs the verified timing on any shot, every application and every passing review growing the flow's applied/verified record), sequence programs that cut whole episodes with named grammars (per-slot fx and physics bindings, a per-slot WIND call the robes ride, a per-slot CLOTH call and FLESH call that scale the cloth and soft-body solvers' answers for the whole shot, and a per-slot MOTION call that CHAINS A PERFORMANCE - a choreography preset, a built-in, or a LEARNED MOTION FLOW whose verified timing re-performs on every shot the slot stamps, so the whole sentence re-performs end to end: lens, air, solvers, world AND body) plus LEARNED SEQUENCE FLOWS the studio directs from per dramatic register (`learn_sequence_flow` adoption, `direct_sequence register:'<register>'` consult - the consult itself names the verified programs no flow carries yet, and the chain rides the consult too) and `stage_battle` points the chain at EPISODE-SCALE battles (THE BATTLE IS STAGED: an arc of register legs - BATTLE > PURSUIT > RESOLVE - each leg consults the register's best-proven flow, the episode's shots are allocated across the legs by each sentence's own weight, EVERY leg pre-flights before a single shot is stamped, and the chained performances ride the slots) and the **Manifest** call-sheet panel, and pixel-level render judgement (review_render -> render_fix -> render_redirection). |
| **Render pipeline** | **Real animated shots, not stills**: every render job finishes as a sequenced h264 clip driven by the shot's camera grammar (movement, shot type, lens, lighting) and the scene's live parameters (fog veil, deterministic lightning flashes, energy pulses, rim contrast) - plus **character motion inside the frame**: shots carry start/end POSES (STANCE, WALK, LUNGE, SLASH, CAST, DRAW, BLOCK, LEAP, CROUCH, FALL, RISE, BOW, POINT) that the engines interpolate across the clip, so the character performs the beat, not just the lens. Blender is a NATIVE capability now: the studio provisions its own binary when missing, runs a resident headless bridge server it health-checks and restarts, and designs a versioned ASSET LIBRARY (.blend characters and environments with previews and honest identity scores) that render jobs load instead of rebuilding procedural stand-ins. Two engines produce those clips behind one job lifecycle: a **headless Blender worker pool** (a locally installed `blender` binary is auto-detected; each job spawns a fresh Cycles-CPU worker and `ANIMEOS_RENDER_WORKERS` slots render CONCURRENTLY - when the pool is full a PREVIEW overflows to MOTION while a FINAL parks in a priority wait queue with an honest 3D-WAIT stage) and the **built-in MOTION engine** (ffmpeg camera program over the shot's AI key art, or a procedural gradient plate when no art exists yet; overflow renders fall here while the 3D pool is busy). A workstation Blender can still attach over HTTP (`ANIMEOS_BLENDER_HOST`); without any engine a wall-clock simulator keeps the lifecycle intact. An **opt-in img2vid PREVIZ slot** (demoted to default-off: the output is designed, not AI-generated) lets pose-carrying hero shots preview their beat as an interpolation animatic when explicitly enabled (`ANIMEOS_IMG2VID=on`) or a host is attached (`ANIMEOS_IMG2VID_HOST`) - a previz pass and a motion benchmark, never the final render path; its job cards read PREVIZ. **Per-state pose presets** give the vocabulary muscle memory: every development state carries a start/end pair (`create_character_state` auto-lands the library preset matching the label - a furious state performs STANCE → LUNGE, a grieving one folds STANCE → CROUCH - and `set_state_poses` steers it), landed on any shot in one click (panel inspector's *Use state poses*, or the shots API `applyStatePoses`). The mouth program carries a **neural viseme pass**: an LLM plans the phoneme-level shapes (the mouth identity no envelope can recover) and conforms them onto the audio envelope (which keeps ownership of openness and timing), so consonants land where the words put them - and an **acoustic-model slot** (deterministic local DSP, `ANIMEOS_ACOUSTIC=off` disables) re-times the plan from the REAL WAV: the take is segmented into speech runs, silences and syllable anchors, spoken units redistribute over the real speech time (a unit interrupted by a pause continues after it), boundaries snap to the acoustic valleys between syllables, and coverage stops where the voice stopped instead of where the script ended - and a **neural rung** (`ANIMEOS_ACOUSTIC=neural`) adds an ASR pass that HEARS the take and collapses the units of words the voice skipped, so the mouth never performs unspoken words (the transcript is word evidence; the DSP warp still owns the timing). Clips play inline in the queue (every finished job card carries a video player), DSH's `render_shot` result names the engine and the camera move, and **Episode cut export** concatenates every shot's clip in story order, grades the episode's sound SERVER-SIDE - the cues land on KIND BUSES (VOICE / SFX / BGM / AMBIENCE) under a fixed gain law, the BGM ducks under the voice through a real sidechain compressor, the master is measured to the -16 LUFS target (EBU R128, the before/after numbers and the applied gain land in the manifest) and every bus stem persists beside the cut as its own WAV - and muxes video + audio into one distributable mp4 with a JSON manifest - and the **publishing panel** stages that cut for a platform with conformance checks, metadata and subtitle sidecars (packages land as PUBLISH events, upload stays a credential-gated or manual hand-off). Same LLM render evaluator with *bounded* parameter fixes, apply-fixes → auto re-render, approve → FINAL, batch rendering with throttled DSH inspections. **The world answers the beats**: a shot's directed grammar drives the worker's FX pass (TRAIL/BURST/AURA/MOTES emissive rigs bound to grammar beats), the physics pass (KNOCK/DEBRIS/SWAY rigid bodies that ground, roll under friction and settle to rest) and the REACTION pass (the figure staggers, dips and buckles away from the impact while the cloth whips with it) - and the finished clip is JUDGED: a pixel review measures the frame locally and reads it against the shot's directed intent, the fix loop applies the parameter fix each issue kind calls for and re-judges on the fresh pixels, and a graduated re-direction proposes grammar/fx ops when parameters are exhausted - all judged by the same fresh review. |
| **Blender design facility** | The studio designs like a 3D facility: a four-kind ASSET LIBRARY (CHARACTER / ENVIRONMENT / PROP / CREATURE - spirit swords, artifacts, vessels, quadruped/serpent/bird beasts) built from the production's design text into versioned .blends with lit previews and animated loops; NAMED DESIGN LAWS reused by name and usage-counted (design_material Principled BSDF recipes, design_lighting rigs, design_motion performances, design_variation Geometry Nodes scatters and arrays, design_sculpt seeded value-noise carves, design_fx spectacle programs, design_physics body laws); the SELF-CORRECTING DESIGN LOOP (design_audit's deterministic local audit + vision critique lands per-criterion scores and a concrete issue registry; design_fix runs a real bpy refinement pass, saves a new version and re-audits - an issue closes only when the re-audit agrees); LEARNED CRAFT MEMORY (learn_retopo_flow remembers verified topology runs - adoption, consult, reinforcement; plan_sculpt's surface probe + vision read plans the carve before it happens and the named SculptPlan grows its measured record; learn_sequence_flow adopts a verified sequence program as a dramatic register's named flow - direct_sequence consults the best-proven one, every application that lands whole with a clean read earns a clear, and the consult itself names the proven-but-unadopted programs so the memory grows on proposal); SCULPT AND BUDGET (per-kind triangle budgets, blender_retopo collapse-decimate with verified drift); and GLB/FBX ROUND-TRIP (blender_export re-imports every export and verifies it PER-CHECK: mesh, material and bone NAMES, the action's frame range, UV sets, bounded bbox/tri drift - an unverified export is a hope, not a deliverable). |
| **Style direction** | Per-production **art style tuning**: a custom style directive (overrides the visual-style preset), palette tokens and extra negative tokens - compiled into every panel-art and model-sheet prompt, tunable from the studio UI or by DSH itself. |
| **Casting consistency** | Per-character **AI model sheets** (turnaround reference images) plus a stored **canonical visual anchor** - the exact prompt tokens re-injected into every panel featuring that character, keeping faces/wardrobe coherent across panels and episodes. |
| **Continuity engine** | Universe-level conflict detection (e.g. destroyed artefact reappearing in Ep 29) with proposed resolutions, missing-capability analysis per scene, and **art-aware continuity**: a deterministic scan flags panel art that predates the episode-active state it should carry or the current model-sheet anchor (stale art), plus featured characters without an anchor; a **VLM deep check** sends a hero panel against the character's canonical model sheet and lands ART_DRIFT / ART_VERIFIED continuity events; **universe-facts vision checks** judge panels against the production's canon rules with a per-fact confidence (FACT_HELD / FACT_BROKEN events) feeding a worst-first re-render queue; and **identity-similarity scoring** puts a number on each panel's resemblance to its cast's model sheets (IDENTITY_VERIFIED / IDENTITY_DRIFT events, a below-bar re-paint queue on the Continuity view) - all surfaced in the Continuity view and to DSH via `check_art_continuity` / `check_universe_facts` / `score_panel_identity`. |
| **Comic Mode** | Shot breakdowns re-composed as sequential art - **manhua** pages, **manhwa/webtoon** vertical scroll, **manga** right-to-left pages. Deterministic panel-layout engine, **AI-generated panel artwork** (style-aware prompts seeded with shot type, environment, weather and character states), **speech-bubble authoring** (speech / thought / SFX, RTL-aware placement), print/PDF export, and **webtoon slice export** - the strip re-rendered at 800px and packed boundary-aware into platform-ready PNG slices, each scored slice carrying an **audio stem** (16-bit WAV, cues re-timed onto the slice timeline) that mixes **real TTS voice renders** for dialogue cues (**per-artist voice casting**: a character's cast roster artist performs the line; uncast speakers fall back to deterministic casting; **per-character-state delivery**: AUTO resolves the speaker's episode state, e.g. a battle-damaged Lin Yue reads strained and slow while an excited read rushes at 1.18x; a **line-level delivery** authored per dialogue line outranks scene direction, so one shot can play neutral then injured mid-performance; and a pinned standing direction overrides the state; a **state arc** (dialogue-editor arrow buttons or DSH `set_state_arc`) stamps one state onto the speaker's every following line across the scene, or across scene boundaries through the end of the episode (`scope:'episode'`), so a whole beat performs possessed without line-by-line edits, and each shot card carries violet **arc-span chips** (with the full span shown in the panel inspector's State arcs section: range, cross-scene badge, position and the covered lines); **state speed/pitch hints** bend pace and voice depth on top, so a possessed read can be slower and deeper without anyone re-pinning a register) with the synthesized SFX/BGM/ambience beds, plus a manifest with artists, LoRA metadata, voice takes (cast artist, delivery, direction + resolved state per take) and audio timing. The manifest also tags every voice stem and take with its **direction currency** (fresh / stale / unrendered against the current direction, per stem and per take, so downstream tooling knows which speech needs a re-render), and an **export pre-flight** names each stale or blocked take before the ZIP downloads, with an explicit export-anyway override. A **direction diff** board re-resolves what every VOICE cue in an episode would render as today, diffs it against the snapshot each take was made with, and re-renders only the affected takes. **Arc storytelling at season scale**: a **season-wide arc ruler** draws every state arc as a violet bar over an episode axis with **one lane per speaker** (a labeled gutter row per character, spans that survive an episode boundary merged into ONE season arc, click a bar to open that episode, clickable episode labels focus the ruler per episode), and **arc templates** (built-ins "possession spread", "full takeover", "recovery arc" plus **user-defined shapes saved per production or in the studio library**, with shape **versioning** that archives every replaced shape) paint a reusable beat shape onto one speaker's lines - or onto SEVERAL speakers as one parallel beat - from a dialog with a live dry-run preview, a **version diff** and a **cross-scope diff** (any two shapes across built-in / production / studio side by side with a human "what moved" list, plus **per-scope usage counts** on every saved shape). The same shapes are paintable and suggestable inside DSH via `apply_arc_template` / `suggest_arc_template`. **Everything with stored takes plays**: arc cards and ensemble clusters in the panel inspector (a cluster shows the merged story-order queue and streams the whole beat), ▶ chips on the ruler bars, and playable arc chips inside the DSH reply itself - all fed by one per-episode playback feed. |
| **Multi-artist studio** | **Style LoRA registry** (per-adapter trigger tokens, strength, base model) with **per-shot fine-tuning** from the panel inspector and **simulated LoRA training runs** distilled from approved panels (live steps, decayed loss curve, milestone log, TRAINED badge) plus a **Train all** batch button that fine-tunes every idle adapter in one pass; an **artist roster** with per-shot assignment, bulk assignment, a **workload-balance view** (production-wide spread badge, per-artist bars, **affinity-first pool distribution**: LoRA-bound panels route to the artist with the highest style affinity for that adapter, unbound panels rotate by load, **per-artist style affinity** scoring which LoRA each artist delivered with) and **per-artist voice casting** (each artist carries a TTS voice; characters bound to an artist via the casting board or DSH's `cast_voice_actor` are performed by that artist's voice everywhere), plus **per-state voice variants**: a development state can bind a different TTS voice (DSH `set_state_voice_variant` or the character sheet picker), so a possessed / transformed / battle-damaged Lin Yue is performed by a different voice entirely while that state is episode-effective; each state can also carry **speed/pitch hints** (character sheet selects or DSH `set_state_voice_variant` args) that bend the take's pace and pitch while the state is effective and flag older takes stale. The casting board **auditions** any roster voice on a throwaway TTS render - a custom line, or the character's own first dialogue line - in a chosen register before anything is cast, **auditions a whole state** (its variant voice plus its speed/pitch hints, before binding anything), runs **ensemble try rows** (one batch call renders every picked speaker as an A/B row with a per-row compare and a cast-order sequence play), and keeps a **per-state audition history**: every proposed read (DSH variant binds, ensemble applies, board state tries) lands in a timestamped, replayable, removable list with its performance snapshot (voice, register, speed/pitch, duration, the line, the date) pruned to the latest 12, and **any two recorded reads chain back to back** - pick sides A and B, hit compare, swap the order, hear them one after the other. DSH can staff whole scenes autonomously via `auto_assign_scene_team`. |
| **3D cinematic preview** | Three.js blocking preview built from the production's DESIGN DNA - the same compiled character and environment structures the Blender worker renders from: the cast figure carries the character's hair, robes, accent and weapon energy, the set is the scene's environment (terrace / peak / forest / gorge / temple terrain, moons, pagoda, bell, banners, bamboo, cloud sea, weather particles, time-of-day sky), plus live scene parameters and shot camera presets (movement-aware) with auto shot advance. |
| **Studio UI** | Dashboard, Productions, Characters (states / relationships / derivatives), Story & Scenes, Comic Mode, Timeline, Manifest (the director's call sheet: sequence programs, learned flows, and every shot's beats, poses, world bindings and render state), Render Queue, Reviews, Continuity, Terminology, Subtitles, History. |
| **Multi-user studio** | Shared-workspace auth with **role-gated writes**: register (first account becomes OWNER automatically), sign in at `/signin`, and every member sees the same productions while the role gates WRITES - VIEWER is read-only (the route gate refuses every mutating API call with an honest 403), EDITOR directs DSH and edits productions, OWNER manages the roster (the header user menu's Studio roster panel: members, liveness readouts, role selects) with the last OWNER protected. Two enforcement layers: the Next 16 proxy gates pages behind a session and mutating APIs behind a role above VIEWER, and sensitive routes re-read the user row fresh from the DB so role changes land immediately. **And the workplace landed**: comment threads anchor to episodes, scenes, shots (a comic panel IS its shot), voice takes and canon facts - the anchor row resolves the project server-side, EVERY signed-in member may speak (VIEWERs ride a one-path proxy allowlist: speaking is not directing), the thread's author or EDITOR+ resolves, and each comment lands a COMMENT event. The **human approval gate** (OWNER policy toggle on the Render Queue) turns DSH's APPROVED inspection into a recommendation: the render parks at awaiting-creator until an EDITOR+ approve releases it to FINAL, or a reject sends it back with a required note that lands in the shot's thread as a first-class comment; the **Reviews** view gathers what waits on people (gate-held renders with one-click approve/reject, open crew threads), and the DSH context's workplace line carries the gate state and the open threads so the director reads the room before it directs. **Per-production memory boundaries**: DSH directs one production at a time - its conversation, canon and events stay bounded to it; a mid-turn create_project keeps the conversation on the home production, opens the newborn's history with an origin event and seats its maker on the newborn crew. **Invite links**: the OWNER's keyring cuts single-use codes that register a newcomer with a carried role (EDITOR/VIEWER) and an optional crew seat with craft lens - spent keys, revoked keys and expired keys all open nothing. |

## Native format support

| Format | Pipeline posture |
|--------|------------------|
| **Donghua** | Style preset with zh-CN terminology defaults, cultivation-state character modelling, ink-wash flavoured panel art, xianxia demo universe. |
| **Anime** | Cel-shading posture, anime timing stage in the render ladder, ja-JP terminology defaults. |
| **Manhwa** | Manhwa-inspired framing preset (ko-KR) + webtoon vertical-scroll Comic Mode. |
| **Manhua / Manga** | First-class Comic Mode layouts with correct reading direction (LTR / RTL / vertical). |

## Architecture

```
src/
├── app/
│   ├── api/            # 15 route handlers (projects, characters, scenes, shots,
│   │                   #   render-jobs, dsh, panel-art, character-sheet, bridge, …)
│   └── page.tsx        # single-route studio SPA
├── components/
│   ├── views/          # studio views (dashboard, dsh-console, comic, …)
│   ├── preview/        # three.js cinematic preview
│   └── studio/         # app shell (zustand + TanStack Query)
└── lib/
    ├── dsh/            # tools, prompts, orchestrator, evaluator
    ├── ai/             # art service: panel art + character model sheets
    ├── bridge/         # MOTION engine (ffmpeg camera grammar) + Blender bridge
    ├── blender/        # NATIVE runtime (provisioning + resident server + exec) + asset library
    ├── engine/         # render pipeline (Blender ⇄ MOTION ⇄ simulator drivers)
    ├── comic/          # panel layout, dialogue model, stems, episode cut builder
    ├── continuity.ts   # conflict + capability checking
    └── seed.ts         # "Immortal Path" demo universe
bridges/blender/       # animeos_bridge.py - worker-mode renderer + attachable server
```

**Stack:** Next.js 16 · TypeScript · Prisma/SQLite · TanStack Query · zustand · Tailwind · shadcn/ui · Three.js.

Per the replaceability principle, the render engine is a **pluggable driver** chain: headless Blender workers when a `blender` binary exists, the built-in MOTION engine (ffmpeg) otherwise, a wall-clock simulator as the last resort - the production state machine, queue and DSH evaluation loop are identical.

### Render engines

- **Headless Blender (auto-detected).** If a `blender` binary is on the machine (or `ANIMEOS_BLENDER_BIN` points at one), each render job spawns a fresh worker: `blender -b -P bridges/blender/animeos_bridge.py -- --worker --job <file>`. Since v4.0 the worker renders the DESIGNED pass: AnimeOS compiles the production's design text (model-sheet anchors, appearance notes, wardrobe/weapon states, environment briefs) into design DNA (`src/lib/animation/design.ts`) and sends it with every job, so the worker builds the DESIGNED character (stylized smooth figure, layered robes with sleeves, cuffs, sash and skirt panels, hairstyle per DNA - topknot / ponytail / braid / long / short, per-DNA weapon - sword / staff / spear - with the character's energy color, high collar, glowing stylized eyes) on a DESIGNED set (terrain per environment - terrace / peak / forest / gorge / temple - plus features: two moons, pagoda silhouette, bronze bell, wind banners, pillar stubs, bamboo, sea of clouds, lanterns, waterfall and stream, with a time-of-day sky and key light; a second detected character blocks across the set). A prop-scale camera table frames the 0.9m figure properly (chest/face-height targets, 1.9x distances). Without DNA the worker falls back to the v3.3 stand-in figure and set. It builds the set and drives a camera rig through the shot's movement grammar frame by frame, applies the AnimeOS scene parameters, renders with Cycles CPU and encodes h264 (system ffmpeg, or Blender's own FFMPEG writer). Shots carrying a pose program get an **articulated stand-in figure** whose joints interpolate between the start/end poses with eased timing - lunges, casts, walk cycles with stride swing and root bob - and the rig follows the subject's travel so the body stays framed. And since iteration 74 the body can PERFORM instead of slide: a keyed choreography program (design_choreography / set_shot_choreography - built-ins The Combo, The Draw Storm, The Rising Fang) replaces the two-pose interpolation with keys - anticipation earns the strike, the strike explodes, the hold sells the read, the follow-through settles - while a REAL impact light flares and the camera takes a decaying punch on the strike frame and the striking limb smears up to 1.35x on the fastest frames (the keys own the body; the lens, the cloth, the flesh and the physics still answer it). The v3.2 rig adds a **face and hands**: emissive eyes that squint and blink on a deterministic schedule, brows that knit angry or lift surprised, a mouth that opens with effort, and on each arm a palm, four fingers and a thumb that curl into grips, spread to channel energy or extend an index to point - every pose carries a 7-channel face/hand row eased between the endpoints exactly like the joints, and tight shot types aim the camera at the head so closeups frame the performance. The v3.3 rig makes the face **speak**: a shot with SPEECH dialogue and a tight framing arrives with a millisecond viseme program and the mouth performs the lines frame by frame - vowels open, m/b/p press shut, "ee" spreads, "oo" purses, and the pose's effort mouth stays as the floor. The program is **audio-driven over the real TTS take**: the take's WAV is analyzed frame by frame (RMS energy opens the mouth, zero-crossing rate separates voiced vowels from wide fricative hisses, silence closes), so the lips match the audio the viewer hears; the v3.4 program adds the **neural viseme pass**: an LLM plans the dialogue's phoneme-level mouth shapes (a 15-entry vocabulary - round oo, wide ee, lips-shut stops - weights clamped, cached per line set) and the plan's identity is CONFORMED onto the audio envelope, which keeps ownership of openness and timing, so consonants land where the words put them; without a take the plan performs the line itself, and a refused model degrades to the audio-only then text pass without blocking the render. Since iteration 75 the workers form a POOL (`ANIMEOS_RENDER_WORKERS`, default 2, clamped 1..4, read live): two Cycles workers render CONCURRENTLY, and when the pool is full a PREVIEW overflows to the MOTION engine while a FINAL PARKS in a priority wait queue (FINAL over PREVIEW, older first, an honest 3D-WAIT stage line) until a slot frees - the tick pumps the queue within one poll, and a waiter lost to a server restart fails honestly instead of hanging.
- **MOTION engine (built-in).** A deterministic camera program (per-movement zoom/pan/tilt profiles x shot-type framing x lens character x lighting grade x fog/lightning/energy) is animated over the shot's key art with ffmpeg zoompan - or over a procedural gradient plate when no art exists - and encoded at the project's fps. Pose pairs play here as a blocking approximation: the program notes them and lands an impact beat at the end pose. Speaking closeups get a blocking speech beat per spoken line (a soft shimmer over each line's window) so the cut still reads as "someone is talking here".
- **img2vid previz slot (opt-in, default off).** Demoted: the studio's output is designed, not generated, so the interpolation model is previz and benchmark only. When explicitly enabled (`ANIMEOS_IMG2VID=on|zai|previz`) or a host is attached, pose-carrying hero shots route to it: the shot's key art (inlined as a data URL) plus a pose/camera program go to the async video-generation API; on a speaking closeup the prompt also carries the lines as explicit speech direction; the finished animatic downloads back into `public/renders/` and plays in the same queue behind a PREVIZ chip. An attached host wins (`ANIMEOS_IMG2VID_HOST` speaking the tiny jobs protocol); unset flag and no host means the slot is invisible and the designed engines (Blender, MOTION) take every job. A provider lost mid-job degrades to the MOTION engine transparently (and rebuilds the speech program so the takeover keeps the lip-sync).
- **Native Blender runtime + asset library (v4.1).** The studio owns its Blender: `src/lib/blender/runtime.ts` detects the binary, auto-provisions the pinned release (Blender 5.2.2 LTS from download.blender.org since iteration 72 - the proven 4.3.2 stays as a fallback candidate) when a workspace reset wipes it, starts a RESIDENT headless bridge on :8101 that it health-probes and restarts with backoff (the per-job spawn path stays as fallback), and exposes an exec sandbox (`blender_exec`) where the DSH designer runs real bpy passes. The design-time library (`src/lib/blender/assets.ts` + `bridges/blender/asset_builder.py`): one design DNA in, one versioned .blend + a lit 512px Cycles preview out (`assets/blender/`, previews served from `/assets-blender/`), a `BlenderAsset` row per character/environment carrying version, build log, DNA audit and an honest vision identity score vs the canonical sheet. READY assets ride every render payload; the worker appends the .blend and resolves the SAME v3.x/v4.0 rig contract by base object name, so poses, lip-sync and camera math are untouched - a missing file falls back to the procedural DNA builders honestly, and the worker state reports `figureSource` / `setSource` so every job's asset decision is auditable.
- **Workstation attach.** Run the bridge server inside any Blender (GUI or headless) and point `ANIMEOS_BLENDER_HOST` at it: jobs go over HTTP and the finished clip returns as base64.

```bash
# inside any Blender ≥ 3.x (GUI or headless):
blender -b -P bridges/blender/animeos_bridge.py -- --port 8100
# then point the studio at it and restart the dev server:
ANIMEOS_BLENDER_HOST=127.0.0.1:8100 bun run dev
```

The add-on exposes `GET /status`, `GET /progress`, `POST /render`, `POST /ping` on `127.0.0.1`. Every engine maps the tunable scene parameters (fog, lightning, energy, rim light, camera distance) onto its own equivalents, honors the shot grammar (ESTABLISHING→24mm wide … EXTREME_CLOSEUP→100mm), streams per-frame progress into the same queue UI, and lands an mp4 in `public/renders/`. If an attached bridge drops mid-job the driver chain takes over transparently.

## Run it

```bash
bun install
cp .env.example .env      # DATABASE_URL=file:./db/custom.db
bun x prisma db push
bun run dev               # http://localhost:3000
```

The database auto-seeds on first request with the **Immortal Path** demo production: a donghua universe with character development states, a six-shot Scene 12 breakdown, a seeded continuity conflict (Jade Sword destroyed in Ep 29) and terminology entries.

### Try the loop

1. Open **Render Queue**, trigger a render on any shot - the **Engine driver** card shows whether headless Blender workers or the built-in MOTION engine is driving; the job badge shows `BLENDER`, `PREVIZ` (the opt-in previz slot) or `MOTION`. Watch progress stream per frame, then **play the finished clip right on the job card** - a dolly-in on a closeup dollies, a pan pans. Give the shot a **pose program** first (panel inspector > Motion poses, or DSH `set_shot_poses`) and the clip shows the character performing: the Blender stand-in lunges, casts or walks between the start and end poses. Or skip the authoring: hit the panel inspector's **Use state poses** and the featured character's episode-active state lands its preset (a furious state performs STANCE → LUNGE).
2. Same view, pick an episode in **Episode cut** and hit *Export cut (mp4)*: the episode's clips concatenate in story order, the stems synthesize server-side (real TTS takes included) and video + audio mux into one downloadable cut with its manifest. Then pick the episode in **Publishing** and hit *Stage package*: the cut is conformance-checked against the platform preset (try Douyin with a 16:9 cut for the honest FAIL), and the package carries the title/description/tags, the SRT subtitle sidecar and the upload checklist.
3. Watch the evaluation come back `NEEDS_REVISION` with bounded parameter fixes → **Apply fixes** auto-queues attempt 2.
4. Open **DSH Director** and talk to the studio: *"For scene 12 shot 3: author the dialogue, generate a model sheet for any new character, then paint the panel in manhua style"* - the 81-tool trace shows the director doing it itself. Ask it to *"shift to a cold moonlit silver-blue palette"* and it retunes the production's style direction itself. Ask it to *"land the beat breakdown for episode 1"* and a per-episode template lands as a proposal in the plans panel. Ask *"how is the studio doing?"* and `studio_pulse` answers with the canon score, identity standing, drift curves, schedule outcomes and queue pressure. Ask *"catch me up"* and `post_digest` lands a digest of the last 24 hours in the studio digest panel.
5. Open **Characters** - hit ✦ on a character to generate their **model sheet**; the stored anchor then steers every panel they appear in (cards show an `ANCHOR` badge once locked).
6. Open **Comic Mode** and flip the same episode between manhua / webtoon / manga layouts. Bubbles (speech / thought / SFX) place themselves around the art - mirrored for manga RTL - **Print / PDF** exports pages with chrome hidden, and in webtoon format **Export slices** downloads a ZIP of 800px platform-ready strips whose audio stems mix real TTS voice takes into the score.
7. In **Comic Mode**, open **Motion sound** on a motion panel: auto-score the timeline, hit **Render voices** to give every dialogue cue a real TTS take performed by the speaker's cast artist in the speaker's current character state (or pick a voice, speed and an explicit excited / injured delivery per cue, plus a directorial note), and preview - exported stems use the same takes. Author **line-level delivery** in the dialogue editor (the ✎ on any panel) to play one shot's lines in mixed registers; the motion-sound board shows which line direction outranks the standing one.
8. Open **Artists** to run **voice casting**: give each roster artist a TTS voice, then bind characters to the artist who speaks for them. **Audition** any voice first - the board renders a throwaway sample (your line, or the character's own first line, in a chosen register) without saving a take - or pick a development state under a character and hear exactly how that state performs (variant voice + speed/pitch hints). Every proposed read lands in the state's **audition history** (replayable, removable), and **any two recorded reads chain back to back**: pick sides A and B, hit *compare A · B*, and hear them one after the other. Select several characters for an **ensemble try** and DSH or the board renders an A/B row per speaker with a sequence play. Ask DSH to do the same with `cast_voice_actor`, and to set standing deliveries scene-wide with `direct_voice_takes`.
9. After a state beat or a line edit, open **Direction diff** in the Comic Mode toolbar: every take is compared against today's direction and **Re-render affected** touches only the stale ones (fresh takes are never re-rendered).
10. Open **LoRA** and hit **Train all** to batch-fine-tune every idle adapter from the approved panels (each run shows a live loss curve), or **Train** a single one; then open **Workload** and hit **Distribute pool (affinity-first)** to route unassigned panels to the artist whose style affinity is highest for each panel's adapter.
11. Back in **Render Queue**, use **Batch render**: tick two episodes, hit *Queue N renders*, and watch the queue fill, DSH inspect each preview (throttled per poll), and the stats row track active / review / approved. Finished cards carry the **per-provider readout** ("MOTION 12.4s (built-in ffmpeg)", ~credits when a hosted provider billed) and the **provider ledger** aggregates latency and cost per engine above the queue.
12. Open **Continuity** and scroll to **Identity similarity**: after panel art exists for sheeted characters, *Score worst 3* vision-scores the panels against every featured character's model sheet - rows show per-character percentages, anything below the 60% identity bar lands in the re-paint queue with a one-click *Re-paint + re-score*, and the *Affinity pass* button embeds every panel and sheet locally (block-robust region hashes + palette histograms, instant and provider-free) to keep a drift tripwire alive when the vision provider is down. When a character's drift curve DECLINES because the design itself moved, press **Re-anchor** on their curve row: the canonical sheet regenerates, an IDENTITY_REANCHOR event marks the baseline restart (the dashed violet marker on the sparkline), and their recent panels re-score against the new sheet. Just below, **Canon health** rolls the universe-facts verdict history into a score with per-fact status rows. In **DSH Director**, the plans panel's **Per-episode templates** section lands a shaped plan (beat breakdown, panel pass, render pass, canon audit) for the picked episode - approve it, run it by hand, or arm a nightly schedule and the studio breaks the episode down while you sleep - and asking DSH for a `studio_pulse` reads the whole studio's health (canon score, identity and affinity, 14-day schedule outcomes, queue pressure) in one instant line.
13. Sign in as a second member (register from `/signin` - they start VIEWER) and the studio becomes a WORKPLACE: every render card's **Discuss** button opens the shot's comment thread where any signed-in member can speak (VIEWERs included - speaking is not directing), the thread's author or EDITOR+ resolves, and the new **Reviews** view collects the crew's open threads. Arm the **Human gate** (OWNER toggle on the Render Queue): DSH's APPROVED inspection then PARKS the render at awaiting-creator with a HELD badge and an *awaiting approval* counter until a person approves it into FINAL or rejects it with a note that lands in the shot's thread - and the DSH context's workplace line carries the gate state and the open threads, so the director reads the room before it directs.

## Status

The full thesis runs end-to-end: DSH directs dialogue, panel art, model sheets, style direction, scene staffing, voice casting and state-aware voice direction through 80 production tools (including the NATIVE BLENDER DESIGNER: the studio's own auto-provisioned Blender runtime with a resident health-checked server, a design-time asset library of versioned .blend characters/environments built from the design text, previews, and honest vision identity scores - ready assets ride every render payload so the worker loads the DESIGNED asset instead of rebuilding procedural stand-ins, including the supervised auto re-paint runner, the universe-facts loop with reword-or-retire suggestions, cross-turn plans, per-episode plan templates plus creator-authored variations, the cadence scheduler with a daily digest, identity scoring with per-character drift curves, and the studio_pulse health readout); the acoustic-model slot's evidence is now PERSISTED per take (a one-click acoustic audit records the speech runs, syllable anchors and the neural rung's ASR word evidence on the cue row, badged on the sound timeline), reworded facts close their own loop (a reword records the old wording, and the drift sweep re-audits every pending fact in one pass, clearing the marker when the loop closes), publish packages now write their HAND-OFF FOLDERS to disk (package.json + SRT + checklist next to the cut) and a PUBLISH_RUN schedule stages an episode's package on a cadence, and identity vision-scoring now judges the SHIPPING PIXELS too: a frame pulled from each finished render clip is scored against the cast's model sheets alongside the storyboard panel, so the panel-versus-render identity gap is measured, not guessed; renders are real animated clips - a headless Blender worker pool or the built-in MOTION engine animates every shot's camera grammar into an h264 cut that plays straight on the queue card, speaking closeups lip-sync their lines on the stand-in's mouth rig with the program derived from the real TTS take's audio and CONFORMED to a neural phoneme plan (the LLM plans the phoneme-level mouth shapes - round oo, wide ee, lips-shut stops - the audio envelope keeps ownership of the timing, and iteration 67's FORCED ALIGNER places every phone to the millisecond (words land on the take's measured syllable anchors, each phoneme window measures its own openness from the audio, and the plan's closed stops cap the mouth in their exact windows - persisted per take on the acoustic audit's phoneme timeline; without a take the plan performs the line itself, and a refused model degrades to the audio-only then text pass), and episode exports mux those clips with server-synthesized stems into one distributable mp4; work that outlives a turn lands as a resumable plan the creator approves in the review panel and DSH executes step by step across conversations, recurring work runs on a cadence without anyone clicking - nightly plan fires land the breakdown steps, a render-queue watch starts re-paint passes when the universe queue is dirty, and every fire is auditable as a production event - the studio's engines report per-provider latency and estimated cost on every queue card with a ledger per engine, and the studio reports its own health and writes to the creator: a canon score over the fact verdict history with reword-or-retire suggestions for facts whose audits keep failing, per-character identity drift curves AND per-fact confidence drift curves over episode order, a 14-day schedule digest with overdue and error-streak flags, a provider-free embedding tripwire (block-robust region hashes + palette histograms per panel vs every sheet - a reframe moves the global hash but not the blocks) under the vision identity scores, and a daily digest (scheduled or on demand) that lands the last window of studio activity as one readable message and DELIVERS itself beyond the studio - POSTing to a webhook and mailing via SMTP when ANIMEOS_SMTP_URL is configured, every outcome recorded on the digest; Comic Mode produces AI-illustrated, dialogue-authored, casting-consistent pages in all three native formats, directs voices line by line inside a shot, auditions cast choices and whole character states before committing them, remembers every proposed read per state with a replayable A/B-chainable history, exports webtoon platform slices whose audio stems carry cast-artist, character-state-shaped TTS voice takes over the synthesized score, diffs every episode's direction and re-renders only the takes that moved, measures the whole season's state arcs on a cross-episode ruler with one lane per speaker, paints beats from reusable arc templates (saved per production or in the studio library, versioned, usage-counted, diffable across scopes), lands ensemble beats on several speakers in one batch with same-turn auditions and playable arc chips in the reply itself, batch-trains style adapters from approved panels, and routes the panel pool affinity-first while ranking per-artist style affinity, and the queue swallows whole episodes in one batch; and the studio is a WORKPLACE now - comment threads hang off episodes, scenes, shots, voice takes and facts with every signed-in member able to speak (VIEWERs included, through a one-path allowlist) and the author or EDITOR+ resolving, an OWNER-armed human approval gate turns DSH's APPROVED inspection into a recommendation that waits for a person (a reject's required note lands in the shot's thread), a Reviews view gathers what waits on humans, and the director's context carries the gate state and the open threads so it reads the room before it directs. The Blender frontiers turned the studio into a DESIGN FACILITY (iterations 51-61): the asset library grew to four kinds with designed material/lighting/motion/variation/sculpt recipes, the self-correcting design loop audits and fixes every design with persisted per-criterion evidence, per-beat secondary motion hangs every figure's cloth and hair on the directed grammar beats - and iteration 65 graduated the CLOTH to the real Blender solver: the wind calls, beat-boundary impulses and the body's stagger steer each part's anchor bone and the Armature-Subsurf-Cloth stack answers with real folds and lag no spring can fake (hair keeps the springs; the render state names which ran), and iteration 66 dressed the render from the sheet itself: the canonical sheet's measured palette is COLOR LAW over the DNA defaults (a bounded 35% pull on robe/accent/hair/boots, honest skips, the state's identity line), named FX programs ignite the beats (trail, burst, aura, motes), named physics programs answer them with real gravity, bounce and settle, REACTION staggers the body away from the violence, sequence programs cut whole scenes with named grammars (now whole episodes, with per-slot fx and physics bindings), the finished render is JUDGED by a pixel review that measures the frame and reads it against the shot's directed intent, the fix loop closes on the fresh pixels and graduates to cited grammar/fx re-direction when parameters run out, and the craft is LEARNED: verified retopo runs become named flows, and the surface probe + vision read plan every sculpt carve before it happens.

## Roadmap - what is not achieved yet, and how we will get there

The plan's destination is an AI-native studio that takes a story from prose to published, animated episodes with zero manual coordination. The thesis loop is closed, but these parts of the vision are still open:

1. **Performance-grade character motion.** Characters move inside the frame: shots carry start/end pose chips (13-pose vocabulary), every development state carries a **pose preset** (auto-resolved from the label, steerable per state, landable on shots in one click), the Blender worker articulates a stand-in between the poses with a **full face and hand rig** (blinking emissive eyes, tilting brows, an effort mouth, and fingers that grip the blade, spread to channel or extend to point, with blade follow-through), the **img2vid previz slot is DEMOTED to opt-in previz** (default-off: the output is designed, not AI-generated; `ANIMEOS_IMG2VID=on` or an attached host previews a pose beat as an animatic behind a PREVIZ chip, never as the deliverable), the MOTION engine plays an impact-beat approximation, and **speaking closeups lip-sync**: the mouth rig performs a viseme program **derived from the real TTS take's audio** (RMS energy opens the mouth, zero-crossing rate separates voiced vowels from wide fricatives, silence closes; the dialogue-text program remains the fallback when a take is missing), the img2vid prompt gets the lines as speech direction, and MOTION lands a blocking speech beat. The **neural viseme pass** landed: an LLM plans the dialogue's phoneme-level viseme score (a 15-entry mouth-shape vocabulary, weights clamped, cached per line set) and the audio path CONFORMS to it - openness stays audio-owned while wide/round identity and closed-stop placement come from the plan; without a take the plan performs the line itself (a strict upgrade over the per-character table), and a refused model degrades to the audio-only then text pass without blocking the render. And the **acoustic-model slot landed**: the take is segmented deterministically into speech runs, real silences and syllable anchors, the plan's units re-time over the real speech (a unit a pause interrupts continues after it, boundaries snap to acoustic valleys, coverage stops where the voice stopped), so the mouth shapes land where the VOICE put the words - `ANIMEOS_ACOUSTIC=off` keeps the plan's even spread. What is still missing: learned interpolation quality on par with the best closed video models (timestamped phoneme alignment landed in iteration 67: a deterministic forced aligner places every phone to the millisecond from the plan's shapes, the take's measured energy and the DSP's syllable anchors - words spend the anchors their vowels demand, and a bilabial stop the plan placed closes the mouth in its exact window)). And **solver-grade FLESH landed** (iterations 65 + 68 + 69): the robes are SOLVED (the real Blender cloth solver under the directed air, its per-shot CLOTH call scaling the answer) and the flesh layer itself is now SOFT BODIES - the trunk and face volumes run the real Blender soft-body solver (probed before it shipped: storm lag 141x the calm hold, bit-exact determinism, bounded, settling), driven by the same beat clock, with the per-shot FLESH call directable beside the cloth one. What remains open here is learned interpolation quality on par with the best closed video models.
2. **Continuity checking that sees the universe, not just the sheet.** The art layer joined the continuity engine: a deterministic scan flags stale art (panel predates the episode-active state or the current model-sheet anchor) and missing anchors, a **VLM deep check** compares a hero panel against the character's canonical model sheet (ART_DRIFT / ART_VERIFIED events), and **universe-facts vision checks** judge every panel against the production's canon rules with a confidence per fact - confident violations rank into a worst-first re-render queue. The loop now CLOSES ITSELF: every panel prompt carries the production's active canon (**fact-aware art prompts**), the **supervised auto re-paint runner** walks the queue worst-first without the clicks - each step re-paints with the flagged facts restated as corrections, re-checks with the vision model, records FIXED / STILL_BROKEN / ERROR, and still-broken panels stop retrying and wait for the director (pause / resume / abort between steps, run state persisted and streamed to the Continuity view) - panels carry an **identity score**: one vision call scores the panel against EVERY featured character's model sheet (0..1 per character plus face/hair/wardrobe/weapon/palette/style aspects), persists per shot, and a worst below the 60% identity bar queues the panel for a one-click re-paint + re-score (IDENTITY_VERIFIED / IDENTITY_DRIFT events, DSH `score_panel_identity` targeted, worst-first batch or default-to-worst) - and the whole history reads back as a HEALTH READOUT: the **canon health** panel rolls the fact verdict history into a 0..1 score (0.4 coverage + 0.6 hold rate over active facts, an unchecked canon is not a healthy canon) with per-fact status rows (HELD / VIOLATED / UNVERIFIED, worst-first) - and the panel now SUGGESTS ITS OWN FIX: a fact audited on 3+ panels whose hold rate fell under a third earns an **auto-retire suggestion** (worst first, the reason names the counts and the reword-or-retire fork), with Reword (PATCH the text in place) and Retire (deactivate) buttons in the canon-health panel and the suggestion riding `studio_pulse` and the DSH context. The curves went FACT-LEVEL: every verdict becomes a point on a per-fact confidence curve over episode order (same trend bands as the character curves, steepest decline first), DECLINING facts surface in the canon-health panel with inline sparklines, `studio_pulse` and the DSH context name them - a fact losing verdict confidence across the show is headed for a violation streak, and the reword-or-retire suggestion is the exit - and the **reworded-fact re-audit helper** closed the loop: rewording a fact (Reword button, or the canon-health API) automatically re-runs the vision check on every panel that audited the OLD wording, so those same panels answer whether the NEW wording holds, the fresh verdicts feed the health score and the drift curves, art-less targets are reported as honestly skipped, and everything lands as a CONTINUITY event - and the suggestions are CURVE-AWARE now: a fact whose confidence curve DECLINES steadily across episodes earns the reword-or-retire suggestion even while its hold rate is still perfect, so the writing fix lands one episode earlier. The reword loop went SELF-SERVE: a reword records the OLD wording on the fact (`lastRewordedFrom`), and the **reword drift sweep** (canon-health panel button, `reaudit-drifted` action) re-audits every pending fact in one capped pass - a fact closes when its panels were actually re-checked under the new wording, or vacuously when nothing ever audited the old wording (a marker that could never serve would never clear). Next: a reword-aware curve break marker.
3. **DSH across turns and days.** The director used to reason only inside one 4-round turn. Now work that will not fit lands as a **cross-turn plan**: create_plan writes an ordered tool-call list (each step with a why, capped at 12) that starts PROPOSED in the creator's **plan review panel** on the DSH view; approving opens the runner and run_plan executes the next 1-3 steps per call through the real tool path (a failed step parks the plan AT that step for a retry), with pause/resume/abort steering and every result recorded on the plan plus production events - and the plan's progress rides the production context, so a LATER conversation picks it up exactly where it stopped. The studio now also RUNS ON A CADENCE: `create_schedule` arms recurring work (a PLAN_RUN walks an approved plan maxSteps per fire, so nightly breakdowns execute themselves once the plan is approved; a REPAINT_QUEUE watch ticks active render jobs and starts a supervised re-paint pass when the universe-facts queue is dirty and no run is live), an in-process minute loop fires due schedules between conversations, each fire claims its slot first (no double-runs) and lands a production event with its outcome (OK / SKIPPED with the reason / ERROR) on the row for the creator to audit, and DSH steers schedules with `create_schedule` / `steer_schedule`. The nightly-breakdown pattern now has a ONE-CLICK entry: the plans panel carries **per-episode plan templates** (beat-breakdown opens a fresh scene with a three-shot cinematography breakdown, panel-pass paints and scans a scene's first three panels, render-pass queues previews plus a direction diff, canon-audit vision-verdicts the hero panel against the facts); landing one resolves the episode's real scene/shot numbers into a PROPOSED plan the creator approves once and runs by hand or arms on a nightly schedule (UI, DSH `land_episode_plan`) - and the creator can now author VARIATIONS: the plans panel's author clones a built-in's resolved steps, reshapes every row (tool, args JSON, why), saves it to the production or the studio library with `{episode}`/`{latestScene}`/`{title}` tokens that resolve per episode at landing, and lands it by name or id exactly like a built-in (tools validated against the registry on save and again at landing, usage-counted). And every engine now reports **per-provider latency and estimated cost**: each render job records a telemetry span per provider that worked on it (takeover chains preserved), hosted providers bill documented per-clip-second credits while local compute bills zero, the queue card reads the chain back ("MOTION 12.4s (built-in ffmpeg)"), and a provider ledger aggregates jobs, average/total latency and credits per engine. The cadence also reports its health now: a **schedule-health digest** aggregates the 14-day fire history per schedule (OK / skipped / error counts, consecutive-error streaks, OVERDUE flags for armed schedules whose next fire slipped) into chips on the scheduler panel, a line in the DSH context and a `studio_pulse` segment. And the studio WRITES TO THE CREATOR: a **DAILY_DIGEST schedule** posts a digest of the last 24 hours (renders queued, plan steps executed, schedule fires with error counts, canon and identity-drift headlines, queue pressure) as a DIGEST production event the studio digest panel shows with its full section lines and history, `post_digest` lands one on demand, and the digest panel has a one-click Arm nightly digest shortcut. And the digest DELIVERS ITSELF BEYOND THE STUDIO now: a DAILY_DIGEST schedule carries `webhookUrl` / `digestEmail` targets (validated at registration, steerable any time via the digest panel's Delivery editor or a PATCH), every fire POSTs the digest JSON to the webhook and mails it via SMTP when `ANIMEOS_SMTP_URL` is configured, and every delivery outcome - OK or the honest FAILED, including "no SMTP transport configured" - is recorded on the digest event and rendered as chips on the digest cards. And the delivery spine grew a LAST MILE: `publish_cut` stages platform publish packages (YOUTUBE, BILIBILI, DOUYIN, TIKTOK, STUDIO_INGEST) - the episode's latest cut is probed and conformance-checked against the preset (duration cap, canvas/aspect - a 16:9 cut honestly FAILS a 9:16 platform, file size, frame rate, bitrate), the package carries clamped metadata, a provenance credits line and an SRT built from the episode's dialogue timed through the cut manifest, and the integration status is honest (credentials present, or a manual hand-off checklist) - NO network upload ever happens silently; packages land as PUBLISH events the render view's publishing panel lists, each staging now WRITES its hand-off folder to disk (`public/renders/cuts/packages/EP01/<platform>/`: package.json + the SRT + checklist.txt - the folder is what gets zipped for ingest or dragged onto an upload form), and the cadence scheduler gained a **PUBLISH_RUN** kind: a schedule pins an episode + platform and stages its package on a cadence (conformance checks and hand-off folder included, next fire computed, every outcome a production event). What is still missing: per-variation schedule presets and richer webhook templates; the adapters speak the platforms' documented upload flows and read credentials from the environment, so real-account OAuth walks are the remaining step.
4. **Voice scale and character voices.** TTS is a fixed catalog of voices with rate-limit ceilings (the E2E suite backs off on 429s), and voices are cast, not cloned - UNTIL NOW for the clone half: the **voice-clone slot** landed (`train_voice_clone`, the characters view's Train-voice button): a character's rendered takes (up to 6 of their real performed audio) go to the configured cloning provider (`ANIMEOS_VOICE_CLONE_URL`), the trained voice id persists on the character, and their lines perform with THEIR voice through the provider - a state voice variant still deliberately overrides, the catalog voice is the honest clone-fallback (stamped as such in the take's signature so diffs stay truthful), and every refusal (no provider, no takes, provider error) is reported instead of pretending. What is still missing: a provider abstraction with retry/backoff plus render caching keyed on voice + text + delivery + speed/pitch, and rate-limit headroom for the catalog.
5. **Evaluation that measures consistency.** Render evaluation is LLM-graded on composition, the vision-model identity score puts a number on each panel's resemblance to its cast's anchors, and a **provider-free embedding distance** now runs underneath it: every panel and sheet is embedded locally (a 64-bit dHash for structure plus a 4x4x4 palette histogram, through sharp, zero providers), compared by cosine + hamming into an affinity per character, persisted per shot and surfaced as chips on the identity rows, a rider on every DSH identity score, an instant *Affinity pass* button and a watch line when the vision provider is rate-limited (the tripwire honestly never replaces the vision verdict). **Per-character drift curves** now join the affinity history: every identity score becomes a point on a character's curve over episode order (story-ordered points, delta and trend band at 5 points - DECLINING / IMPROVING / STABLE / FLAT), the weakest aspect across the curve is named, declining characters surface as a watch in `studio_pulse` and the DSH context, and the identity panel draws each curve as an inline sparkline so a slide across the SHOW is visible at a glance. And identity scoring now looks at the SHIPPING PIXELS: every finished render clip donates a poster frame (ffmpeg, 40% into the clip, cached per job), and the same vision verdict path scores it against the cast's sheets as a separate RENDER-source row beside the PANEL score (drift curves stay panel-canonical so a shot does not double-count) - the storyboard passing while the render drifts is now a number the studio can see. What is still missing: a render path that carries the designed identity faithfully enough to clear the 60% bar (the v4.0 designed render pass - robes, hair, weapon and set compiled from the same design text the sheets were painted from - closes most of the stand-in gap; the stylized-procedural figure still scores low against painted sheets, honestly). Iteration 66 attacked the COLOR half of the gap: **THE SHEET DRESSES THE RENDER** - the hero's canonical model sheet is measured into a deterministic palette (quantized clusters, bit-stable across reads) and the figure's robe, accent, hair and boots pull a bounded 35% toward their nearest cluster before frame one (a color already true to the sheet skips with its name, a missing sheet is honestly absent, the render state reports the conformed materials and the law) - and FINAL renders now render with real light bounces instead of the flat zero-bounce look. The **design-time asset library** attacks the same gap from the other side: characters and environments are DESIGNED once into versioned .blend assets (with an asset-level vision score: build, inspect, refine, re-inspect - the design loop raised the cast's asset identity before a single render was spent) and every render loads the asset. The tripwire is now COMPOSITION-ROBUST: every panel and sheet carries, beside the global 64-bit dHash, a 3x3 grid of per-region 16-bit dHashes and 2x2 quadrant palette histograms; blocks whose neighbour-difference signal is below the flat gate abstain, the survivors vote and the vote pools by MEDIAN, so a reframe (a crop-shift, a subject that drifts a block over) holds at 100% block agreement where the global hash drops to 78% - proven in `scripts/check-block-affinity.ts` - while a real grade drift still reads clearly lower than a faithful panel. And the identity loop CLOSES ITSELF now: the **re-anchor workflow** (identity panel button, `reanchor_character`) regenerates a DECLINING character's canonical sheet from their CURRENT design text, lands an IDENTITY_REANCHOR event that restarts the curve's trend baseline (a dashed violet marker on the sparkline; old points stay as history), and re-scores their recent panels against the new sheet in the same call - re-paints fix panels, re-anchor fixes the reference, and doctrine rule 21 teaches DSH the difference. Iteration 59 landed THE RENDER IS JUDGED - the learned assist layer on the deterministic passes: a finished render is no longer inspected TEXT-ONLY, it is JUDGED. A pixel review (`review_render`, tool 75) pulls a representative frame from the finished clip (the ffmpeg poster, 40% through), MEASURES it locally with a deterministic sharp pass (luminance mean and spread, saturation, near-black and clipped-white fractions, a 16-bin histogram - the same bytes always land the same numbers, proven across runs), then shows the frame to the vision model TOGETHER WITH the shot's DIRECTED INTENT (the grammar beats, the fx and physics kinds the production put on the shot) - a cinematographer watching dailies, not a photo critic. The verdict is a persisted `RenderReview` row with per-criterion scores (exposure and contrast measured locally, readability/palette/intent/composition judged against the direction), concrete issues (EXPOSURE | CONTRAST | READABILITY | PALETTE | INTENT | COMPOSITION | STAGE, with severities and cited numbers), the provider named honestly (vision+local | vision | local), and ONE review per render job (the pixels do not change until a new attempt renders new ones - the second call reuses the same review). The DSH inspection (`runRenderEvaluation`) now runs the review FIRST and reads the pixel evidence in its prompt (EVIDENCE, not a gate - the inspection still decides), the review's MAJOR issues ride the inspection findings, the context gained a pixel line, and the render card grew a cyan PIXEL REVIEW strip (state, overall %, provider, criteria chips, the reviewed frame thumbnail beside the video). Doctrine grew THE RENDER IS JUDGED (the tenth design law) and rule 37 (JUDGE THE PIXELS - never approve what nobody watched). E2E `scripts/e2e-iter59-render-review.ts`: 61 checks ALL GREEN twice over the real runtime (synthetic dark/flat/gradient frames judged with cited numbers and bit-exact re-measurement, honest refusals with no clip, a REAL directed render reviewed - metrics measured, intent carried, provider honest, frame on disk, the SAME review reused, the inspection completing with the review as evidence, the queue API riding the review to the card, the role matrix with the OWNER unblocked, exact cleanup); regression suites iter49-58 ALL GREEN (51-55, 56-58 updated to registry 75); browser E2E: the directed job card shows the PIXEL REVIEW strip with a clean console.
6. **A studio with more than one person in it.** The door landed first (multi-user auth with role-gated writes: bcrypt accounts, first registration becomes OWNER, VIEWER read-only enforced by the Next 16 proxy on every mutating API call, EDITOR directs and edits, OWNER manages the roster from the header's Studio roster panel with last-OWNER protection, and sensitive routes re-read the role fresh from the DB so changes land immediately). And the WORKPLACE landed: comment threads anchor to episodes, scenes, shots (a comic panel IS its shot), voice takes and canon facts, with the anchor row resolving the project server-side (the client never declares it) - EVERY signed-in member may speak through a one-path proxy allowlist (speaking is not directing), the thread's author or EDITOR+ resolves, every comment lands a COMMENT event, and the DSH context's workplace line surfaces the open threads so the director reads the room before it directs. The HUMAN APPROVAL GATE joined the pipeline: an OWNER arms it per production (a DB-fresh policy toggle on the project PATCH), DSH's APPROVED inspection then PARKS the render at awaiting-creator (the evaluation persists, so the tick never re-claims it) until an EDITOR+ approve releases it to FINAL or a reject sends it back with a required note that lands in the shot's thread as a first-class comment; doctrine rule 25 teaches DSH the gate is human (never route around it - make the case in the reply instead), and the Reviews view gathers what waits on people: gate-held renders with one-click approve/reject beside the crew's open threads. And the studio grew PER-PROJECT MEMBERSHIP SCOPING: a ProjectMembership table (unique per project+user, cascade both ways) makes each production a SILO - a member sees exactly the productions whose crew they are on (the picker, the Productions cards and every project-scoped API route resolve through one DB-fresh `requireProjectAccess` gate, ~40 routes wired including child rows resolved through their relation chains), the OWNER carries NO row and passes BEFORE any membership lookup (implicit, global, unconditional - the studio's administrator is never blocked from any corner, proven by a 28-endpoint owner sweep with zero refusals), the project list filters itself per slate, a creator lands a DIRECTING membership on what they made, and the Crew panel on each production card lets an OWNER add/remove members and set each member's craft lens while members retune their own (the proxy's second deliberate exception - a lens is not a permission). And every dashboard now EMPHASIZES ITS READER: a new `/api/studio/emphasis` endpoint reads the real rows into a per-role payload (OWNER: the studio-wide overview - every production, the roster's liveness, the gate; DIRECTING: story funnel + DSH's latest direction; ART: identity drift + panel coverage; VOICE: casts + takes + auditions; REVIEW: approval queue + open threads), and the dashboard renders the caller's lens first with role/craft chips, honest empty states for a fresh VIEWER (nothing on your slate yet) and an unblockable OWNER. And the studio learned WALLS AND KEYS (Iteration 50): DSH's memory is bounded per production - doctrine rule 26 (ONE PRODUCTION, ONE MEMORY) teaches it, the orchestrator enforces it (a turn's conversation persists to the production it STARTED on even when create_project switches mid-turn, the newborn's history opens with an origin event naming who and where it came from instead of a borrowed half-conversation, and DSH-made productions seat their creator on the crew with a DIRECTING lens exactly like the API path does), and the studio grew DOORS THAT HAND OUT KEYS: an `Invite` table + `/api/invites` keyring (OWNER-only) cuts single-use codes that carry the role the newcomer arrives with - EDITOR or VIEWER; ownership is earned in the roster, never invited - and, when the key names a production, a crew seat with a craft lens; registration resolves the key BEFORE creating the user and spends it in the same transaction that seats the member, so revoked, expired and already-used keys open nothing (410); the OWNER cuts keys from the user menu's Invites panel or straight from a production's Crew panel, and `/signin?invite=CODE` pre-aims the sign-in form at registration with the banner up. Iteration 68 made the floor VISIBLE: every member-driven tool call now carries MEMBER ATTRIBUTION (an AsyncLocalStorage actor context stamps the session user onto the production-event ledger), **presence** classifies the roster into online / recent / away (polling IS the heartbeat - the session touch keeps the caller live), **member digests** build one honest footprint per member from their attributed events and their crew-thread comments (a quiet member is reported honestly - a digest is never invented; `post_digest member:'<name>'` posts one on demand), and the dashboard's **Studio floor** panel shows presence dots + digest lines per member, refreshed each minute. The auth stack migrated to **next-auth v5 (Auth.js)** (iteration 70) and the migration killed the last open advisory chain: nodemailer left the dependency graph as a bundled v4 dep and returned as the studio's own patched v10 email transport - `npm audit` reads **0 vulnerabilities** (24 with 2 criticals at the audit pass's start). And the still-missing line above is now landed too: **per-member outbound notifications** (iteration 71) - each member subscribes their own channels on the dashboard's *My outbound* panel (a public-webhook endpoint under the SSRF law, and/or email to their account over SMTP), digest posts and release slates fan out to the subscribed crew + OWNERs, and every attempt lands on the NotificationDelivery ledger (SENT / REFUSED / FAILED with the detail) - a notification nobody received is a row, not a silent drop.
7. **Distribution beyond the file.** Webtoon slices, page BOOKS (server-assembled **CBZ** with ComicInfo.xml carrying the manga RTL binding, and **PDF** with panels composed at their real grid placements and a ViewerPreferences Direction hint for right-to-left reading), print PDFs and episode cuts download locally, subtitles translate through the Terminology memory into any language (glossary-enforced, suggestions grow the memory), and the delivery spine STAGES platform publish packages (conformance checks, clamped metadata, SRT sidecars) and now UPLOADS them: `upload_package` runs each platform's real adapter (YOUTUBE resumable init+PUT, TIKTOK/DOUYIN init-then-put, BILIBILI multipart submit) when the environment carries the credential, with every outcome recorded on the package's PUBLISH event.
