Next: a release calendar and real-account OAuth walks.

8. **A DESIGNER with a Blender runtime, not a prompt with a render button.** The studio's Blender became a place the DSH DESIGNS in, at the depth a professional 3D facility would recognize: the asset library grew from two kinds to FOUR (characters, environments, and now PROPS - swords, sabers, spears, artifacts, vessels, relics built as named part hierarchies with emissive rune placement, and CREATURES - quadruped, serpent and bird archetypes with horns, spines, wings and glowing eyes), the studio's materials and lights are DESIGNED LAWS not per-build guesses (`design_material` registers Principled BSDF recipes that are law over the DNA defaults, `design_lighting` registers key/fill/rim/camera rigs that drive the preview render, both consumed by name and usage-counted), and the builder frames every preview by the asset's REAL bounding box (no more cropped swords or cameras inside serpents). THE SELF-CORRECTING DESIGN LOOP is the heart: `design_audit` judges a design against a quality bar (a deterministic local audit that always runs - hierarchy floors, preview content, designed-recipe presence, identity recency, version churn - plus a vision critique of the preview when the provider answers, landed as persisted per-criterion DesignReview rows and a concrete DesignIssue registry with severity), `design_fix` compiles a REAL bpy refinement pass from the open issues (finishing bevels + subsurf, normal and origin hygiene, roughness/metallic normalization, palette pull toward the accent), saves a new VERSION, re-renders the preview, RE-AUDITS, and marks an issue FIXED only when the re-audit stops raising it - the re-audit is the judge, honestly. `design_status` checks back on the whole standing, the DSH context carries a design line (library, presets, latest audit, open issues), doctrine rules 27-28 and a DESIGN MASTERY curriculum teach the craft (silhouette first, hierarchy of detail, material intent, palette discipline, light logic, genre production grammar, the quality bar), READY props and creatures ride every render whose shot text names them (the worker loads the .blend at a deterministic foreground anchor), and the render view's asset card grew the loop as UI: quality grades, open-issue chips and Audit/Fix buttons beside every preview. Iteration 55 gave the surfaces their finishing passes (item 10's sculpting/retopology frontier, now landed as the deterministic SCULPT + TOPOLOGY laws). The learned layers landed too (iterations 60-61: `learn_retopo_flow` memory + vision-guided `plan_sculpt`, see item 13) - what remains here is deeper ML assist (a vision model that edits geometry directly, learned UV packing).

9. **The designed assets PERFORM - a real rig, a real performance, a real loop.** Iteration 52 closed the biggest gap between "designed" and "production-grade": a statue is not a donghua asset. Every PROP and CREATURE now ships with a PERFORMANCE: `design_motion` registers named motion presets (the third design law beside materials and lighting - hover/spin/pulse for props, slither/flap/walk/prowl/breathe for creatures, with speed, amplitude and loop length), and `blender_asset_build` with `motion:'<name>'` runs the new motion rig (`bridges/blender/motion_rig.py`): a REAL Blender armature is laid out per archetype (a 7-bone chain along the serpent's coil plus neck and head bones, 4 two-bone legs in trot pairs plus spine, neck, head and tail for quadrupeds, twin wing bones plus body, neck, head and tail for birds, a PerfRoot bone under every prop), the asset's named part hierarchy is RIGIDLY BOUND to the bones (an empirical bind that parents, measures and compensates, so world positions survive no matter the pose-matrix conventions), and the performance is BAKED as pose-bone keyframes into a looping Action - whole wave cycles per loop, keys running to frame N+1 so the CYCLES fcurve modifier repeats with no seam, phase offsets down every chain (coils in sequence, legs in diagonal trot pairs, the head leading the neck), the spirit glow breathing on the emissive materials. The saved .blend carries the armature + Action to every consumer: the builder renders the ANIMATED PREVIEW LOOP beside the still (24 frames at 288px through the worker's proven ffmpeg chain, landed as `/assets-blender/{id}.mp4` and PLAYED on the asset card with a motion chip), the design loop grew the MOTION criterion (0.13 of the grade - a motionless prop or creature raises a MAJOR issue, and `design_fix` on it BAKES the archetype default performance for real: rig, bind, bake, loop, version bump, re-audit clears the issue only when it agrees), the DSH context's design line reports performing vs MOTIONLESS counts, doctrine grew the MOTION IS DESIGN craft rules and rule 29 (motion is judged by watching the loop, never by imagining it), and RIDING RENDERS carry the performance live: the worker anchors the rig's ROOT objects (never the bone-bound meshes, so bindings survive) and a shot naming a performing asset plays its designed move inside the frame. The v5.0 quadruped builder also had a latent material-shadowing bug (the Underbelly mesh shadowed its material, crashing every quadruped build) - caught by the motion probe and fixed. Iterations 53-54 closed this item's frontier (shot-level motion grammar, then per-beat secondary motion: cloth and hair RIDE the directed grammar beats), and Iteration 57 closed the last line: physics-accurate prop interaction - a directed KNOCK strikes a riding designed prop (or a declared vessel) with real gravity, bounce, friction and settle on the same beat clock (item 10).


10. **VARIATION, INTEROP, and a DIRECTED lens - the studio designs like a facility, ships like one, and blocks like one.** Iteration 53 landed the three remaining production-grade pillars. VARIATION IS DESIGN (the fourth law): `design_variation` registers named layout laws compiled into REAL Blender Geometry Nodes trees (`bridges/blender/variation_nodes.py`) - a seeded SCATTER across a carrier surface (Distribute Points on Faces, seeded, so the same spec always lands the same sixty DISTINCT rocks) or an ARRAY along a deterministic Python-generated spine (sword racks, colonnades, grids); `blender_asset_build` with `variation:'<name>'` attaches the tree INSIDE the .blend, the modifier travels to every render, the builder reports the VARIATION_SUMMARY (carrier, source, live instance count from an atomic depsgraph snapshot), the audit grew the VARIATION criterion (0.09 of the grade - a wallpaper environment raises a MAJOR issue and `design_fix` bakes the default environment scatter), and the design context line reports the varied standing. INTEROP IS DESIGN (the fifth law): `blender_export` exports any library asset to GLB or FBX and VERIFIES the round trip (`bridges/blender/roundtrip.py` exports, wipes the scene, re-imports the file and compares meshes, triangles, materials and bounding-box dimensions against the source) - every export lands a persisted AssetExport row (verified flag, drift %, the full report with honest format notes: glTF realizes the GN variation into geometry, GLB leaves lights/cameras behind by design, FBX re-triangulates ngons and cannot carry a GN tree so it exports the clean base meshes), the files serve from `/exports/`, and the render view's asset card grew an export strip (GLB / FBX buttons, a VER/UNVER chip, a live VERIFIED note with the numbers). THE GRAMMAR IS DIRECTED: a shot is no longer one camera move held for a whole clip - `design_grammar` registers named blocking language (2..6 camera beats over a 0..1 fraction, each beat one move from the worker's nine-move vocabulary, optionally carrying its own pose pair so the subject moves with the lens), `set_shot_grammar` applies a saved preset, a built-in (The Reveal, The Standoff, The Assault, The Ascent, The Withdrawal) or inline beats, and the worker plays the camera BEAT BY BEAT through a shared framing context (a beat never re-derives its framing) with eased crossfades into the next beat, per-beat poses on the beat clock while global poses keep the global clock, a corrupt grammar degrading honestly to the whole-clip move, and the worker state reporting the beats - the render view shows the beat chips on every directed job. Doctrine grew three curriculum laws (VARIATION IS DESIGN, INTEROP IS DESIGN, THE GRAMMAR IS DIRECTED) and rules 30-31. E2E `scripts/e2e-iter53-variation-roundtrip-grammar.ts`: 88 checks ALL GREEN three times over the real runtime (real GN builds with in-.blend node-tree + live-instance probes, GLB and FBX exports verified by re-import, the wallpaper audit -> fix -> re-audit-cleared lifecycle, a REAL directed render whose worker state reports the beats, and the HTTP role matrix with the OWNER unblocked). Iteration 54 landed the sequence scale: per-beat SECONDARY MOTION (every designed figure's cloth and hair hangs from pivot empties and rides a damped spring whose drive is the DIRECTED beat under the playhead - the beat's own WIND call, the blocking's implied motion energy, the body's pose velocity, and an impulse at every beat boundary so a pose jump across the cut whips the cloth) and SEQUENCE DIRECTING (`design_sequence` registers named cutting programs - ordered chains of grammar slots validated at design time; `direct_sequence` applies one across a scene's shots in order and reads the whole flow back: cut clashes, pose cuts, wind beats, untouched shots reported).

11. **SCULPT IS FINISHING - the surface is carved, the budget is law.** Iteration 55 landed the last deterministic frontier: `design_sculpt` registers the SIXTH design law, named sculpt recipes compiled into layered, seeded VALUE-NOISE DISPLACEMENT carved along the vertex normals (`bridges/blender/sculpt_pass.py`) - swell (broad organic mass), fold (ridged creases and striations), grain (fine tooth) over a single-threaded BMESH-GRID subdivision, deliberately NOT the SUBSURF modifier (OpenSubdiv's parallel evaluation is float-order nondeterministic in the last bits; the seed law demands bit-exact - and the probe proved the SUBSURF path drifted while the bmesh path is identical across runs; the first implementation also carried a latent salt bug: Python's `hash()` on layer names differs per process, handing every launch a different noise seed - replaced with fixed per-kind offsets). `blender_asset_build` with `sculpt:'<name>'` carves the recipe INTO the .blend before the save; the pass MEASURES its own evidence (mean/max displacement the carve applied, Laplacian roughness against the subdivided-but-uncarved SUBSTRATE, tris before/after) and the audit grew the SCULPT criterion (0.08 of the grade - a clean builder slab on an environment or creature raises a MAJOR issue and `design_fix` bakes the kind's default: terrain for environments, hide for creatures, robe folds for characters). TOPOLOGY is a budget, not a vibe: every kind carries a triangle budget (CHARACTER 80k, ENVIRONMENT 120k, PROP 20k, CREATURE 60k), the audit raises a MAJOR TOPOLOGY issue on anything over it, `blender_retopo` runs the collapse-decimate pass on demand and `design_fix` runs it from the issue - both VERIFY the shape survived (bbox drift measured, verified verdict lands with the tris before/after; a budget the ratio floor cannot honor is reported, never faked). A plain rebuild RESETS the sculpt claim (a column naming a carve the file does not carry would be a lie the audit believed). Doctrine grew the SCULPT IS FINISHING curriculum law and rule 33 (MEASURE THE SURFACE - report variance and drift numbers, never a vibe). E2E `scripts/e2e-iter55-sculpt-retopo.ts`: 56 checks ALL GREEN three times over the real runtime (real carves with measured evidence, the bit-exact determinism proof across rebuilds, a creature sculpted past its budget then decimated back to law with drift verified and the re-audit clearing, the audit -> fix -> re-audit lifecycle on both new criteria, the readouts, and the HTTP role matrix with the OWNER unblocked). The learned assist layers LANDED since (iterations 60-61: learned retopo flows + vision-guided sculpt plans, item 13). Iteration 57 closed the physics frontier (item 10): directed KNOCK/DEBRIS/SWAY body laws integrated under the probed bit-exact law.

12. **PHYSICS IS THE WORLD'S LAW - mass answers the beats.** Iteration 57 gave the directed world its solid ground: `design_physics` registers the EIGHTH design law, named body-law programs compiled into REAL rigid bodies integrated per frame (`bridges/blender/physics_pass.py`) under a law PROBED before it shipped (the probe caught a micro-vibration bug - a shallow impact used to bounce forever without settling - and proved bit-exact determinism across runs, the same standard that rejected OpenSubdiv in the sculpt pass). KNOCK strikes a prop when the playhead ENTERS a bound beat: the named DESIGNED prop (matched by name among the riding library assets' anchor empties - the whole prop group flies) or a declared stone vessel takes an impulse velocity away from the figure plus a seeded tumble, then gravity, bounce with restitution 0.32, impact friction, roll friction and settle to REST take over - a re-struck body relaunches, and the report names the frame the wreckage FINALLY settled (an intermediate rest is never passed off as the final one). DEBRIS seeds ten rubble chunks resting in a ring that get kicked radially on bound beats and LIE where they land (physics truth: debris never fades like fx). SWAY hangs a lantern on a damped pendulum driven by the beat's WIND call - the same driver the cloth hangs from - kicked at every beat boundary. The pass rides the SAME beat clock as the camera, the springs and the fx (the beat context is computed once per frame and shared), the seed law holds (mulberry32(fnv1a(job_id) ^ program index)), and the worker state reports the evidence: programs, kinds, boundBeats, strikes, bounces, maxSpeed, settleFrame, maxSwing. `set_shot_physics` applies a saved preset, a built-in (The Clash / The Ruin / The Windchime / The Shove) or an inline array; only KNOCK takes a target (a typo refuses at design time). DSH: tools 73-74 (registry 74); doctrine grew PHYSICS IS THE WORLD'S LAW and rule 35 (LET THE WORLD OBEY ITS OWN LAW - promise wreckage only when the state reports it). UI: the render view's job card grew emerald physics chips beside the amber fx chips. EVIDENCE: E2E scripts/e2e-iter57-physics.ts 72 checks ALL GREEN twice over the real runtime (a designed prop built and knocked by name with strikes/bounces/maxSpeed 5.15/settle reported, the lantern's swing, the cloth riding the same beats, a control render whose stage stays clean, the registry refusals, the context line, the HTTP role matrix with the OWNER unblocked, exact cleanup); regression suites iter49-56 ALL GREEN (51-56 updated to registry 74); browser E2E: the directed physics job card shows CRANE 0-50% W0.9 + DOLLY_IN 50-100% + TRAIL/AURA + DEBRIS/SWAY chips with a clean console; src tsc clean, eslint clean, em-dash clean, production build clean. Iteration 58 closed the ragdoll frontier - THE BODY ANSWERS THE WORLD: the REACTION kind (probed in real Blender first, 14 checks ALL GREEN: real displacement, honest settle with no micro-vibration, bit-exact across runs, Newton's third law, the merged body law, the honest skip) staggers the FIGURE itself on the beat it is bound to - an impulse drives a damped spring on the hero's root (stiffness 46, underdamped 8.5 on purpose: a stagger wobbles then rests), the body lurches AWAY from the beat's violence (when a KNOCK or DEBRIS program fires on the same beat the recoil points away from the struck body; otherwise a seeded dodge direction), dips, buckles (the spine folds, the head lags opposite) and the spring returns the body to its mark, settling to REST with the recover frame stamped honestly (a re-stagger forgets the earlier rest); multiple REACTION programs merge into ONE body law (the figure has one body); the wind has NO force on this law - the cloth answers the air, the body answers the violence. The stagger publishes its velocity on the figure every frame, and the frame loop runs the physics BEFORE the cloth so the robes and hair whip with the body the SAME frame (one body, one cloth - never a frame late). Built-ins grew: The Clash now staggers too, and The Recoil joins (a pure REACTION law). The worker state reports the body's evidence: reactions, maxOffset, maxLean, recoverFrame, boundBeats (a reaction that never fired reports 0). E2E scripts/e2e-iter58-reaction.ts: 64 checks ALL GREEN over the real runtime (the paired law KNOCK+REACTION on the SAME beat - the prop flies and the body staggers as cause and effect, maxOffset 0.067m, maxLean 3.7deg, recoverFrame 63, the cloth riding the same clock, a control render whose stage stays clean, the refusals, the role matrix with the OWNER unblocked, exact cleanup); regression suites iter49-57 ALL GREEN (56-57 updated to the four-kind vocabulary and the new frame order); browser E2E: the directed job card shows DOLLY_IN 0-50% W0.7 + ORBIT 50-100% + KNOCK + DEBRIS + REACTION chips with a clean console; src tsc clean, eslint clean, em-dash clean, production build clean.

13. **THE STUDIO JUDGES, CLOSES AND REMEMBERS - the render is judged, the fix returns to the pixels, the craft is learned.** Iterations 58-61 gave the studio its own eyes and memory. REACTION (the ninth law) staggers the FIGURE itself on the beat the violence lands: a damped spring drives the hero's root - lurch away from the impact, dip, buckle, recover and rest - with the same-frame cloth whip riding the stagger's velocity, and multiple REACTION programs merge into one body law. THE RENDER IS JUDGED (the tenth law): every finished render earns a pixel review (`review_render`) that MEASURES the frame locally (luma mean and spread, saturation, near-black and clipped-white fractions, a 16-bin histogram - bit-exact across runs) and shows it to the vision model TOGETHER WITH the shot's DIRECTED INTENT (the grammar beats with their wind calls, the fx and physics kinds), landing a persisted per-criterion verdict with concrete issues. THE FIX RETURNS TO THE PIXELS (iteration 60): `render_fix` applies the parameter fix each issue kind calls for and queues the new attempt whose fresh review is the judge - an issue is FIXED only when the new pixels stop raising it - and `learn_retopo_flow` turns a verified topology run into the kind's named LEARNED FLOW (adoption, consult, reinforcement - craft that compounds, consulted by design_fix before any budget pass). And iteration 61 read the surface before the carve and graduated the fix: `plan_sculpt` probes the asset's actual .blend (tris, Laplacian roughness, spread, density, a scale-free relative roughness folded into flatness), plans the treatment through the vision model WITH those numbers (an honest local fallback when the provider cannot), compiles through sculpt's boundary law into a named SculptPlan that every carve's measured evidence grows and every re-audit clear reinforces, and `render_redirection` proposes grammar/fx ops that CITE the issue (kind, severity, the review's number) once parameters are exhausted - judged by the same fresh-pixel law, with contrast and palette refusing escalation because they belong to the rig and the materials. Iteration 62 graduated both remaining frontiers: sequence programs now cut WHOLE EPISODES in story order (scope:'episode' allocates the slots across every scene, the flow read names the scene of every cut, and slots bind fx and physics programs so the world answers on the same sentence, validated at design time), and the round trip verifies PER-CHECK - mesh names, material names, armature bone names, the baked action's frame range, UV sets, bounded dims and tris - with the re-import purging orphaned source datablocks so the counts cannot lie (FBX exports with add_leaf_bones=False so bone names survive exactly). Iteration 63 made the studio remember its sentences and made the direction legible: `learn_sequence_flow` adopts a verified sequence program as the production's named flow for a dramatic REGISTER (BATTLE | PURSUIT | REVEAL | STANDOFF | RITUAL | INTRIGUE | RESOLVE), `direct_sequence register:'<register>'` with no program and no slots starts from the register's best-proven flow (a flow that never landed whole is never consulted), every application grows the flow's measured record (shots stamped, wind beats, pose cuts, move clashes, world bindings, renders queued - a direction that lands whole verifies, a clean read earns the clear), and the new **Manifest** view is the director's call sheet: the named programs, the learned flows with their runs and clears, and every shot's beat chain, pose pair, fx/physics bindings and render state, scene by scene, crew-readable. Iteration 64 taught the sequence sentence to call the AIR and the consult to name its teachers: a slot may carry a WIND call over its grammar's beats (a number drives every beat, an array is keyed per beat with null keeping the grammar's own gust and 0 a stillness call - validated at design time against the grammar's beat count, patched onto the shot's beats before the worker plays them, so a directed storm lands as robes and hair answering the sentence, not just the lens), every program-driven direction lands its measured record on the PROGRAM's own ledger (a redesigned sentence resets it - a new sentence is a new lesson), and the consult reads that ledger before it answers: a program that verified but was never adopted is named by the consult itself - in the refusal, in the success read, and in the context's sequenceAdoptions line - and the Manifest's program cards carry the record (runs, landed-whole, unadopted, the flow that carries them), so a proven sentence waits at the top of the call sheet instead of in oblivion. Iteration 65 solved the cloth itself: a real-Blender probe proved the pin convention, bit-exact determinism and a 22x storm-versus-calm tip swing BEFORE the worker adopted the solver; the sash, sleeves, cuffs and all eight skirt panels now run the Armature-Subsurf-Cloth stack with the studio's deterministic air model (wind calls, beat-boundary impulses, the body's stagger) rotating each part's anchor bone, hair keeps its springs, refusals fall back honestly, and the render state names the simmed parts, the springs, the maxAnchorSway and any fallback (the thirteenth design law - THE CLOTH IS SOLVED - and rule 43). Iteration 66 dressed the render from its judging anchor: the hero's canonical sheet is measured into a deterministic palette and the figure's named materials pull a bounded 35% toward the sheet's truth before frame one (skips named, absence honest, the state's identity line the audit), and FINAL renders gained real light bounces (the fourteenth design law - THE SHEET DRESSES THE RENDER - and rule 44). Iteration 67 taught the mouth the millisecond: a deterministic forced aligner maps the phoneme plan's units onto the words they belong to, spends the take's measured syllable anchors on the vowels that demand them, measures each phoneme window's openness from the real audio and caps the plan's closed stops in their exact windows (scarce anchors degrade to proportional placement and say so; the timeline persists on the acoustic audit) - rule 45: the plan owns the closure, the audio owns the energy, the anchors own the clock. Iteration 68 made the SOLVER itself directable and the studio legible to itself: the **CLOTH call** (one number 0..1 per shot or sequence slot) scales the cloth solver's ANSWER - the directed air, the beat impulse, the stagger sway - never its physics (0 stills the solver, absent leaves the full probed response, the render state names `secondary.solver.clothCall`; design-time validation refuses arrays and clamps ranges, a slot without a call clears a stale one, the learned flows keep the call in their sentence, and the render proves it: a storm render at cloth 0.2 still simms all 13 parts with the call stamped in state); **member attribution** rides an AsyncLocalStorage actor context so every production event a member causes names them (the digests are only as honest as their attribution); **presence + member digests** put the roster on the dashboard's Studio floor (online within 3 minutes, recent within an hour, a quiet member honestly quiet) and `/api/studio/digests` + `post_digest member:'<name>'` read what each member actually caused; and the **release calendar** slates episodes for their audience (`schedule_release`, tool 81 - registry 80 to 81, rules 45 to 46; `/api/releases` computes DUE/RELEASED at read time so nothing drifts; the Releases view plots the show on a month grid with a slate dialog for EDITOR+). Iterations 69 to 71 closed the run: **solver-grade FLESH** (the trunk and face volumes are soft bodies under the directed beats - the probed soft-body law answers the same beat clock one solver up from the cloth, with the per-shot FLESH call beside the CLOTH call; the manifest call sheet carries both), the **next-auth v5 (Auth.js) migration** (registry stays 81, rules 43 to 47, `npm audit` to 0), and **per-member outbound notifications** (`/api/notifications` self-service, the dashboard's My outbound panel, the honest delivery ledger). Iteration 72 moved the studio's own runtime up a major line: **Blender 4.3.2 → 5.2.2 LTS** - the pin (`runtime.ts`'s `BLENDER_VERSION_TAG`, the provisioner URL, the bridge's candidate list, the smoke/e2e binary paths), the whole surface re-proven under the new binary before the commit (the bridge worker renders the smoke clip with the v3.2 rig and the FLESH soft-body solver answering; the asset builder's node palette builds its .blend + lit preview clean; the iter-69 FLESH E2E runs ALL GREEN with real directed renders - both solver calls answered by the real Blender), and the resident bridge now runs 5.2.2 with the probed-law comments carrying their re-verification. Iterations 73 to 75 built the three frontiers to Solo Leveling / donghua grade, one thin verified slice each. **73 - THE SKIN IS PAINTED** (character fidelity, part one): every character / prop / creature library build ships painted - deterministic box-projection UVs written in pure Python, one 256x256 texture map per material role painted FROM the law colors (woven threads + dye noise + accent thread for cloth, warm mottle for skin, strand streaks for hair, grain + scuffs for leather and hide; fnv1a + mulberry32, bit-exact across rebuilds - the same DNA always paints the same map), the maps wired as base color and PACKED into the .blend, skin subsurface + cloth sheen where the Blender exposes them; and THE FINAL FRAME IS GRADED - a FINAL render leaves the compositor with bloom + the cool-lift/warm-gain room grade through the 5.x API (CompositorNodeTree on compositing_node_group, interface output + NodeGroupOutput, socket-set parameters, the CPU compositor device - the probe caught the old node_tree/composite-node API dying in 5.2.2 and the GPU compositor's GL requirement), PREVIEW honestly raw (rule 48, the sixteenth and seventeenth law texts). **74 - THE PERFORMANCE IS KEYED** (action performance): keyframe choreography (tool 82-83, registry 81 to 83) - a program of 2..8 keys (at, pose, kind: anticipation | strike | hold | follow | move) replaces the two-pose slide, with a REAL impact light flaring and a decaying camera punch on the strike frame and the striking limb smearing up to 1.35x on the fastest frames; three built-ins (The Combo, The Draw Storm, The Rising Fang); the keys own the body while the lens, the cloth, the flesh and the physics still answer it (rule 49). **75 - THE ECONOMICS** (scale): the serial render law is dead - a POOL of Cycles workers (`ANIMEOS_RENDER_WORKERS`, default 2, clamped 1..4, live-read) renders CONCURRENTLY; when full, a PREVIEW overflows to MOTION (unchanged) while a FINAL parks in a priority wait queue (FINAL over PREVIEW, oldest first, an honest 3D-WAIT stage) that the render tick pumps within one poll, and a waiter lost to a restart fails honestly instead of hanging. **76 - THE MIX IS GRADED** (the nineteenth law, rule 50): the episode cut's audio grew a real mixing console - every cue lands on its KIND BUS (VOICE / SFX / BGM / AMBIENCE) under a fixed gain law (1.0 / 0.9 / 0.55 / 0.4), the BGM bus DUCKS under the voice through a real sidechain compressor (threshold 0.04, ratio 6, attack 12ms, release 320ms), the raw master is MEASURED through EBU R128 and walked to the -16 LUFS target by an explicit clamped gain (+-12 dB) under an honest limiter ceiling, then measured AGAIN - lufsRaw, gainDb and lufsFinal land in the manifest, and every bus stem persists beside the cut as its own WAV plus the graded master; the silence fallback is a hidden intermediate (a cue-less cut does not wear a graded-mix deliverable) and a failed mix removes its stems - the same cue sheet rebuilds to the SAME measured LUFS numbers. **77 - THE MOTION IS LEARNED** (the twentieth law, rule 51, tool 84 - registry 83 to 84): a verified performance is a lesson - `learn_motion_flow` adopts a shot's keyed performance as a NAMED MOTION FLOW for a dramatic register after the ADOPTION GATE verifies it (a pixel review that PASSED its bar, or a human's APPROVED/FINAL - refusals name what is missing), keeping the exact keys, impact and smear that proved themselves; `set_shot_choreography` resolves learned flows beside presets and built-ins (marking the shot's choreo with the flow), every application grows the flow's applied record, and every passing review of a flow-carrying shot grows its verified record; the consult ranks verified evidence first, the refusal registry names the flows with their records, and the context's motionFlows line carries the vocabulary. The iteration's E2E also caught a latent iter-74 bug: an inline JSON choreography program was refused because the raw string never reached the compiler - inline programs parse now. **78 - THE BAR IS MEASURED + THE SENTENCE CALLS THE PERFORMANCE** (the twenty-first law, rule 52, tool 85 - registry 84 to 85): the identity bar is no longer one number for everything - a RENDER is the shipping promise and answers at 70% while the storyboard panels keep the 60% repaint line, and the raise is grounded in MEASUREMENT, not declaration: `measure_identity_bar` walks the production's finished renders, runs the REAL vision identity score on each (a frame from the clip judged against the same model sheets), and reads the honest distribution against the bar - cleared / average / worst / the repaint queue (the E2E measured the painted pipeline's real render-source score live and the tooling carries the number honestly, wherever it sits); a below-bar reading lands IDENTITY_DRIFT on the SHIPPING pixels and earns the re-render; the drift queue, the studio pulse and the Continuity panel all split by source now. And the two learned memories CHAIN: a sequence program's slot may carry a MOTION call (a choreography preset, a built-in, or a LEARNED MOTION FLOW), validated at design time, and `direct_sequence` stamps the shot with the grammar AND the performance through the same flow-aware path set_shot_choreography uses - the chained learned flow's applied record grows, its verified evidence rides the chain, and the consult re-performs the whole verified sentence end to end: lens, air, solvers, world and body. The cut card went through a real-browser E2E (login → episode select → export → the live mix line: measured LUFS, voice-ducked, stems, rendered-inline). **79 - THE CAST ANSWERS THE BAR + THE BATTLE IS STAGED** (the twenty-second and twenty-third laws, rules 53-54, tools 86-87 - registry 85 to 87): identity is answered BY NAME - `cast_identity_pass` rolls the production's real per-character vision readings into one standing per member (CLEARING, BELOW with the worst shot ref named - the re-render queue by name, UNTESTED for anchored-but-never-measured, UNANCHORED for the member with no sheet, who cannot answer at all until `generate_model_sheet` paints one), and the REAL pass ran on the Immortal Path cast: four real sheets generated and anchored, the three cast-bearing shots rendered through the real engine (sheet conformance riding the DNA), the REAL vision measurement landed (avg 8% against the 70% shipping bar - the honest read named Lin Yue BELOW at Sc12 S003 and Demon Lord Wei BELOW, Chen Hao and Elder Han untested, the clone unanchored: the standing IS the work order, the frontier-1 gap is now measured per name, not averaged away). And the chain points at EPISODE-SCALE battles - `stage_battle arc:'BATTLE > PURSUIT > RESOLVE'` consults each leg's best-proven learned sequence flow, allocates the episode's shots across the legs by each sentence's own weight (largest-remainder, deterministic, pure-law in `battles.ts`), PRE-FLIGHTS every leg before a single shot is stamped (a battle that fails at leg 3 leaves nothing half-staged), stamps the grammar + air + solver calls + world + chained performances through the exact resolver set direct_sequence obeys, and lands each leg's measured outcome on the flow's ledger (a leg that could not perform its whole sentence does not verify); the REAL battle staged on episode 7: all 6 shots directed across the three learned legs (coverage 6/6), two built-in performances chained into the clash, 5 cloth calls and 3 wind beats riding the arc, the ledger honestly recording the leg that ran shorter than its sentence. **80 - THE GAP IS REPAIRED + THE DNA ADHERES TO THE SHEET** (the twenty-fourth law, rule 55, tool 88 - registry 87 to 88): the standing's work order EXECUTES now - `identity_repair_pass` works the cast's BELOW list in two halves: (a) each below member's canonical sheet is READ into build DNA by the vision model (`src/lib/blender/adherence.ts` - hair style, exact hexes per material role, weapon, build, beard; cached on the character, staleness keyed on the sheet URL so a re-sheet invalidates it), the render path compiles EVERY detected cast member's DNA THROUGH that read (the sheet's truth wins field by field over the regex guesses; a guess build keeps the gentle 0.35 palette pull, a sheet-adherent build pulls 0.75), their worst named shots re-render over the real engine and the REAL vision channel re-scores them; (b) a member the re-render could not lift has their canonical sheet RE-ANCHORED (regenerated from the current design text) and the shots re-scored against the NEW sheet. The ledger is honest per shot - before -> after with the verdict the pair earns (REPAIRED / IMPROVED / UNCHANGED / WORSE / UNSCORED) - and the IDENTITY_REPAIR production event carries it. The REAL run on the Immortal Path cast worked the named below members: Lin Yue 0% -> 5% and Demon Lord Wei 0% -> 15% (both IMPROVED, both still below the 70% bar - the gap that remains is named, never averaged away), both proxies now built from their sheets' own measured DNA (8/8 fields read per sheet),  **81 - THE SILHOUETTE SHAPES THE MESH + THE SCORE MATCHES THE POSE** (the twenty-fifth law, rule 56, registry stays 88): Frontier 1 went deeper on both halves of the measured gap. The sheet read's silhouette sentence now compiles into a bounded shaping profile (`parseSilhouetteShape` in `src/lib/blender/adherence.ts` - height/shoulders/torso/sleeves/skirt/hair, the build field setting the prior, the note's own words pushing the traits, clamped on BOTH sides of the wire, `fields` naming only what the sheet itself described) and the Blender worker SCULPTS THE PROXY'S MESH with it - shoulder span and sleeve tops, torso/hips bulk, sleeve drop, skirt panels growing DOWN from the same waist line, hair mass, crown presence - mesh only, the rig anchors stay exactly where the framing math and the v3.x rig contract expect them; the render state names the applied factors and the sheet's own words, and a guess build keeps the neutral figure (no profile on the wire at all). And the re-score judges a POSE-MATCHED filmstrip instead of one arbitrary frame: `poseSampleTimestamps` samples the clip at the 22/40/62% marks (a clip too short degrades to one stamp), ffmpeg hstacks the strip, `buildIdentityPrompt` carries the pose-matching law (judge the frame whose pose most closely matches the sheet's canonical turnaround, name it), and the stored note names the artifact judged (`pose-matched over 3 frames; ...` vs `single frame (40% mark)`) - a mid-strike frame scored against a standing sheet measured the POSE, not the character. THE REAL LOOP ran again over the Immortal Path cast at shotsPerMember: 3 with the deepened adherence riding: Lin Yue's E7 Sc12 S002 rose 25% -> 35% and Demon Lord Wei's S004 15% -> 20% (both IMPROVED, shaped builds + pose-matched strips riding, both sheets re-anchored mid-loop with re-scores against the new sheets), S003 honestly WORSE (0%) and named; both members still BELOW the 70% bar - the remaining frontier-1 gap is a modeling-fidelity gap, and the ledger names exactly where it sits. The head is no longer an assembled sphere: `sculpt_head_mesh` displaces a 642-vertex icosphere through eight deterministic bounded laws (jaw taper to the chin, the chin's forward settle, the brow ridge, the eye sockets, the cheekbones, the nose wedge, the skull dome, the ears) and the sheet read's NEW `faceShape` family (oval / round / angular - asked in the same vision read, enum-validated, null-safe) modulates the sculpt through a bounded face profile (`parseFaceProfile` - jawTaper/chinFwd/browFwd/cheekOut/noseLen/eyeScale, clamped on BOTH sides of the wire, the build nudging the jaw, the eye size riding eyeScale); the sphere hair masses became LOFTED strands and volumes per style (`loft_strand` - ring-by-ring spines with tapered tips: the topknot bun + tail, the ponytail sweep + flowing tail, interlocked braid lobes, the long curtain + side locks), all riding the silhouette's hair factor; the rig anchors never move, the render state names the family, the factors, the sculpted parts and the head mesh's DETERMINISTIC HASH (the same profile always lands the same sculpt; a different family lands a different mesh), and a guess build keeps the NEUTRAL sculpt, honestly named. Gates: src tsc clean, eslint clean. **83 - THE SURFACE IS GRADED, NOT PAINTED** (the twenty-seventh law, rule 58, registry stays 88): Frontier 1 went deeper again - the vision model's own re-score notes named the remaining gap ("low-poly 3D mannequin"), a MATERIAL-fidelity gap, not a geometry one, so the likeness is now won in the SURFACE too. Every material is a layered surface built from ONE dye (the sheet read's own measured hex): skin carries subsurface (the soft terminator, radius scaled by the skin tone's own luminance), fine roughness breakup, warm zones (coarse noise pushing the dye toward the warm end) and a fresnel rim (Layer Weight lifting the grazing angle toward the lightened dye); cloth carries the gradient ramp (shadow/high ends derived from the same dye, medium-scale mottle mixing them), the fabric sheen and the weave bump; hair carries the tinted glint (sheen tinted toward the lifted dye) with a tight roughness breakup. The profile compiles from the read's own hexes through `parseMaterialProfile` (bounded: skinSss/skinRough/skinWarmth/rim/clothRamp/clothSheen/clothWeave/hairRough, clamped on BOTH sides of the wire - lighter skin scatters more, a dark robe earns more shadow/high separation, a saturated robe sheens like silk, black hair keeps a tighter glint; `fields` naming only the hexes the read itself owns) and the worker re-clamps against the same bounds (`material_profile`, one law two runtimes); the surface is ALWAYS graded now - a payload without a profile keeps the neutral grade, honestly named (the flat plastic was a pipeline defect, not a sheet trait). THE SHEET DRESSES THE RENDER meets the grade: the palette conformance re-sets a graded material's DYE (`regrade_material` - the whole tree rebuilds from the new hex, ramp ends and zones riding; a legacy flat material recolors its Principled exactly as before), the render state names the applied profile + fields + the DETERMINISTIC grade hash for the hero AND the second figure, and the repair ledger's DNA line grew the grade (`graded: skin sss 1.30 rough 0.48 rim 0.20; cloth ramp 0.24 sheen 0.35 (named by the sheet: skinTone, robeColor, hairColor)`). Iteration 100 (THE COST IS NAMED) gave the renders their ledger: the pipeline had proven the FRAMES right for ninety-nine iterations and never named what they cost - every finished render job carried its measured wall clock and its per-provider telemetry spans and nothing read them back. The Performance Engine (`src/lib/engine/performance.ts`, DSH tool `performance_report`, registry 89 -> 90) earns every finished job a READING: the measured wall (queue, orchestration and render together - what the production actually waited), the frames the job owed by the WORKER'S OWN clamped frame law mirrored bit-exactly (duration 0.8-30s at the default 3.0 with the falsy-0 fallback, fps int-truncated and clamped 1-60 at the default 24, frames = max(2, round(duration * fps)) with Python's round-half-to-EVEN mirrored and cross-verified against the real Python law), the SECONDS PER FRAME spent, the telemetry's own totalMs and the difference named raw as the wait (a garbled telemetry reads honestly - no spans, no total, the wait null); and the budget is NEVER AN INVENTED CONSTANT: each job is judged against its own cohort (the finished jobs of the same driver and mode, comparable by construction) - HOLDS at 2x the cohort's median, SLOW to 3x (the tail, watched), OVER beyond (the regression names itself; a job alone holds its own budget - a regression needs a cohort to be a regression), with nearest-rank p10s riding the matrix's own percentile law; a FAILED attempt measures too (its wall was spent - the status and the fix lineage ride the reading). The E2E crafted a lab at the project's OWN fps 30 (the loader joins the project row) with an eight-job night - a FAILED attempt-2 regression at 9.00s/f named by ref with its 50s wait, a SLOW tail at 6.00, four readings holding, one unfinished job honestly absent - asserted cell-by-cell exact, then read the REAL production's finished jobs over the real night; two crafting bugs were caught by the E2E's own expectations (a 7-element median misread, and a JS half-UP rounding drift on a 37.5s wall the worker's half-EVEN law would never land - re-crafted integral). Iteration 99 (THE DISTRIBUTION IS THE RELEASE) gave the readings their SHAPE: the point statistics could answer average-and-worst, but not where the readings sat - forty at 85% and one at 5% carried the same 'worst 5%' headline as forty at 85% and one at 84%. neighbor phase-a halves re-run ALL GREEN; gates: src tsc clean, eslint clean, py_compile clean Iteration 102 (THE STEM IS REMEMBERED) built the Abstract Audio-Render Cache: a take is a PURE FUNCTION of its structure - the voice, the exact text, the delivery style and the speed/pitch hints - so the first take earned under a structure is remembered in `.cache/audio/` under that structure's composite sha256-16 (`src/lib/ai/audio-cache.ts`), and `renderVoiceTake` looks there BEFORE the provider: an unchanged structure instantly fetches the cached STEM (the post-DSP wav, byte-identical) and its VISEME MANIFEST (the whole-take mouth program stored beside the stem, served on the hit) instead of paying the rate-limited TTS call again; the result now carries the cache evidence (`cache: {key, hit}`) so the routes name what was remembered; a changed structure is a new key (one character of text is structural; sub-hint rounding is not - the take sig's own r2 law) - the cache never guesses a near-miss into a hit; corruption reads as a miss (every read validates the manifest's key, version and byte size against the stem); THE 429 LAW rides beside it: a provider rate limit backs off on the fixed doubling schedule (1s, 2s, 4s) before surfacing - `withRateLimitBackoff` wraps the TTS call, `isRateLimitError` matches the status or the wording and nothing else, and the counters (hits, misses, saves, corrupt reads, rate-limit waits) name the cache's own honesty; the VISEME side of the house got the same discipline: the neural pass's per-span mouth programs are memoized by the take's audio CONTENT + span window (`cachedSpanVisemes`), so re-rendering an unchanged take skips the repeated frame-by-frame DSP. Iteration 103 (THE WORKERS STAY WARM) rebuilt the resident bridge's worker model: v3 spawned a FRESH `blender -b` subprocess per job - a crashed worker could never poison the server and every job got a clean scene, but every job paid Blender's FULL cold boot before the first frame. The pool keeps WARM resident workers instead (`--pool N` on the bridge server, default 2 from `src/lib/blender/runtime.ts`, `ANIMEOS_BRIDGE_WORKERS` overrides, 0 = the cold-only law): each worker is a long-lived headless Blender that loads bpy ONCE, waits for job payloads on a private loopback socket (length-prefixed JSON, a ready-file proving the boot finished so a booting slot is never dispatched), renders through the SAME `worker_run` core the cold spawn uses (the per-frame progress JSON the /progress endpoint polls is unchanged), sends the outcome back BEFORE its clean-scene purge (the next job opens an empty factory scene - the clean-scene guarantee holds; the outcome must never be lost to a purge crash), and waits for the next payload. The server dispatches warm ABOVE the proven cold path: a free warm slot takes the job, a dispatch failure marks the slot dead AND falls back to the cold spawn for the same job file (a dead warm worker never loses a job), dead slots respawn bounded with their lifetime served count preserved; /status names the pool (size/alive/free/busy/served) and `busy` reads true only when no warm slot AND no cold slot is free. Smoke `scripts/blender-pool-smoke.py` ALL GREEN over real renders (one boot, served=2 on the SAME worker, the cold fallback completing a killed worker's job). Iteration 104 (THE SLATE IS SIGNED) hardened the webhook delivery end to end: every outbound webhook payload is a SLATE the receiving target must be able to VERIFY before executing on it - `X-AnimeOS-Signature: sha256=HMAC_SHA256(secret, '<ts>.<body>')` plus `X-AnimeOS-Timestamp` (the replay window is exactly 300s each side; a forged, edited or replayed slate reads refused with the reason named). The secret is the production's OWN: generated once, lazily, persisted on the project row (`webhookSecret`, 32 random bytes hex; `ANIMEOS_WEBHOOK_SECRET` overrides; a delivery without any secret reads honestly 'unsigned', never silently) - stable across fires, so a receiver verifies every slate against one enrolled key. Beside the signature stands the DOMAIN ALLOWLIST: `ANIMEOS_WEBHOOK_ALLOWLIST` names hosts (comma-separated, a leading dot admits subdomains, a `*.` entry too) and the network layer refuses any webhookUrl outside it - at the PEN (`create_schedule` refuses before the schedule even lands) and again at DELIVERY (`deliverDigest` records the refusal outcome without a network call - defense in depth, the same law the SSRF private-host guard obeys, which stands untouched beside it). The verification law (`verifyWebhookSignature`) is what a listening delivery target runs: constant-time comparison, the raw body, the timestamp window. Iteration 105 (THE CLIP-LOSS RACE IS CLOSED) closed the seam the render night caught: the warm dispatch's FLAT 900s socket wait gave up on a HEALTHY worker mid-render (the worker sends nothing until the render concludes), the finalize marked the mid-render job file done with NO error and NO clip, and the tick landed a clipless REVIEW that never re-ticks - a 903s wall counted while the clip was gone. The law, four layers: the wait WATCHES THE WORK (the job file's mtime is the liveness truth - fresh means keep waiting, quiet past WARM_STALE_S means the worker is gone; ANIMEOS_WARM_STALE_S overrides, default 600s), the finalize NEVER LIES (an unconcluded job lands done WITH an honest error - a named failure re-queues, a silent clipless done never does; warm AND cold), the cold budget SCALES WITH THE JOB (600s + 30s/frame from the payload's own clamps - no flat cap kills an honest long render), and the mp4 cache NEVER POISONS (a failed read retries, never a permanent clipless done) - and the spine's own belt: a done answer with no error and no bytes lands a NAMED FAILED, never a clipless REVIEW. The pool base now derives from the server's own port (the smoke on :8133 and the resident on :8101 had competed for the same 9410 workers).
