14. **THE FIGURE IS CRAFTED, NOT ASSEMBLED - the mesh answers the scorer.** Iteration 106 executed the gate's remaining work order: the binding had put the sheets' colors, silhouette and face families on the pixels, but the vision scorer still read the figure as "a low-poly 3D robot" - because the designed body's ORGANIC parts were raw primitives. The craft law: every part the audience reads as cloth or skin is now CRAFTED by the builder (`bridges/blender/animeos_bridge.py`, `src/lib/blender/craft.ts` - one law, two runtimes under a deterministic sha256-16 craft hash): a bevel modifier softens every organic box's hard edges (brows, mouth, skirt panels, sash tail, palms, the sword's guard - the 90-degree edge line that read as machine segments), the boxes smooth-shade, the eight skirt panels take bevel PLUS one subsurf level (a raw box hem read as armor), the ten fingers and thumbs grow as ROUNDED CAPSULES instead of squares (the loudest robot tell - the bevel rounds each cap's rim into a knuckle), and every curved primitive the camera reads lifts to 24 segments (limbs, sleeves, cuffs, sash, the weapon shafts). MESH ONLY: the anchor empties, the pivots, the head sculpt's proven faceHash, the shape keys and the framing math ride untouched - apply_pose, lip-sync and the grip law re-proved green. The craft law's version STAMPS the character asset key (95 -> 106, both runtimes), so the library assets RE-BUILD with the crafted meshes: a craft change is an asset change, and the pixels get it on the asset path too. THE QUEUED SEAMS closed beside it: (1) THE NAME IS A WHOLE WORD - `detectCast`'s substring match read "coiling" as Lin Yue's "lin"; the new law (`src/lib/cast-token.ts`, pure, riding the art prompts, the shots route AND the panel inspector with zero copies) tokenizes both sides and matches whole tokens only - possessives fire ("Lin's blade"), hyphens split ("lin-feng"), case folds, CJK matches by the script law (unspaced scripts have no boundaries, containment IS the word test), a punctuation name honestly matches nothing (the old `includes("")` matched EVERYTHING), and the render of a shot whose only "lin" is "coiling" carries NO cast. (2) THE STATUS NAMES THE PROCESSES - the pool's `/status` bookkeeping drifted under contention because the `dead` flag was memory: an idle worker that died quietly read ALIVE until somebody submitted, and a worker killed MID-JOB stayed busy through its whole cold-fallback window. The law: `/status` and the dispatch path both reap by POLLING the processes first (the flag is memory, the process is truth), busy counts only the living, a new `booting` count names the slots still coming up, the cold fallback rides the cold-lane counter it previously hid from (a saturated pool can never double-spawn cold workers), and a respawned slot's busy flag can never be cleared by the dead generation's cleanup. Smoke `blender-craft-smoke.py` ALL GREEN over real renders (the hash mirrors the independent derivation, every organic box beveled, no 8-vertex cubes in the hands, the 24-segment cap ngons, the anchors at their proven coordinates, the faceHash stable across builds); E2E `e2e-iter106-craftcast.ts` ALL GREEN in phases (the word-boundary grid: the 'coiling' trap, possessives, hyphens, case, unicode, max-3, order; the REAL trap render refusing the cast; the crafted clip naming the craft evidence with the hash matching the TS mirror bit-exactly over the wire; the pool carnage: a killed idle worker reads dead on the next read, the killed mid-render worker never counts busy through the fallback, the fallback holds the cold lane honestly, the job still lands, and the next submit respawns the pool). Neighbor smokes re-proved (grip, character-asset at the moved stamp, pool) + neighbor phase-a halves re-run ALL GREEN (104, 105); src tsc + eslint + py_compile clean.
15. **THE CRAFTED NIGHT BURNED AND THE VISION SPOKE - the distribution names the next frontier.** The render night ran for real over the crafted build (craft law 106 + the bound sheet DNA riding every payload) on the standing production, and the vision re-score judged the pixels with the gate answering on the readings - the release spine's discipline held end to end. THE NIGHT: six PREVIEW jobs over EP07 Sc12 through the resident bridge and the warm Blender pool under the fire's own supervision, the night's cargo directed (S004's aura crawl, S005's slash trace + energy coil, S006's lightning detonation + cloth physics), the character assets compiling FROM THE SHEET READS (10/10 sections, "the sheet read owns hairStyle, hairColor, robeColor, robeAccent, skinTone, weaponType, build, beard") and every cast shot's job file carrying the craft evidence (version 106, 26 softened parts, namedBySheet "oval face, lean jaw" for Lin Yue) - all six shots landed real clips, S006 the castless beat honestly on the MOTION pre-viz, S005 the 'coiling' trap carrying NO cast by the word-boundary law and re-rendering through real Blender. THE NIGHT'S HONEST OPS: the crafted meshes TRIPLED the per-worker Cycles memory (~1.2GB RSS vs ~400MB for the mannequin builds), so the first wave's five-way concurrency (two warm + three cold lanes) was OOM-killed on the 4GB host - three Blender workers and four drill processes died silently, and the night was carried home by the honest recovery loop: the studio's own fail-stale-jobs tool naming the lost workers, the render night's resume law re-enqueueing only the clipless, and the pool sized to one warm worker (ANIMEOS_BRIDGE_WORKERS=1) so every remaining render ran solo or two-way - the ledger's cost class HELD (median 9.09s/f over 14 finished jobs, the crafted best 9.08s/f solo; the Blender cohort median 10.46s/f against the previous night's 11.46s/f - the craft did not blow the render budget), and the fire's cost line read the real walls with the OVER naming the seed's preview-simulator row honestly. TWO ENGINE SEAMS caught on the record and queued: the tick's "bridge lost" degrade branch ignores the job file's concluded state (a done-with-clip job degraded to a clipless SIMULATOR row when the resident restarted between submit and tick - iteration 105's own "the job file is the liveness truth" law not consulted), and the staleness tool reads only mtime (it FAILED a finished-but-unticked render whose clip-ready file sat on disk - wasteful, honest, queued). THE RE-SCORE (real vision calls over the night's real clips): S002 Lin Yue 0.00 + Clone 0.00 ("a 3D robot model that does not match the 2D human character designs"), S003 0.00 ("a dark, faceted vessel with a brush-like top" - the 85mm close-up frames the featureless head; the extracted frame confirms the read exactly), S004 Demon Lord Wei 0.00 ("a 3D mannequin-like figure"), and S005 REFUSED outright - the word-boundary law at the scoring layer: a shot whose only 'lin' is 'coiling' has no cast, and the anchor-required refusal fires. THE VERDICT: BELOW over 5 readings, mean 0%, median 0%, p10 0%, against the 70% floor; the REAL /api/publish route refused verbatim ("An episode does not publish BELOW the floor" with every under cell named), the PUBLISH_RUN cadence SKIPPED on the same refusal, zero PUBLISH events. THE FRONTIER, NAMED BY THE DISTRIBUTION: mesh craft was necessary but not sufficient - the scorer's read is dominated by what the mesh law does not own. (1) THE SURFACE: flat plastic shading drowns the sheet's hexes under the shot lighting - the robe colors ride the DNA but the pixels need cloth materials that carry the sheet palette at render scale. (2) THE FACE: the sculpted face family is unreadable - no features survive at framing distance, and the close-up reads a vessel; the head needs a face that reads as a face where the camera actually points. (3) THE PRESENCE: the figure sits at ~15% of frame height in its own medium shot - the framing and lighting story must put the figure's identity inside the frame. The next work order is the surface-face-presence triad, in that order.
16. **THE TRIAD BURNED THREE NIGHTS AND THE GATE HELD EVERY ONE - the distribution names the body, the light, and the move.** The surface-face-presence triad the crafted night named is now law and was proven on the standing production through THREE honest render-night cycles, each one's pixels teaching the next law. THE SURFACE: the cloth tree carries the sheet's own measured RANGE - the ramp's shadow/high ends pull toward the palette's dark/light members (bounded 0.45) and a large-scale zone wash mixes a mid member across the garment (bounded 0.22), the palette storing on the material so every regrade keeps it; one blended dye drowned under the shot lighting no more. THE FACE: the hair cap's rings center behind the skull and squash along y so the face band stays skin (the old full ring painted the whole head hair - the 'dark vessel'), the fringe hangs above the brow line, the eyes/brows/mouth/nose step genuinely out of the skull (the old eyeball sat 0.5mm inside the surface), the deep carve's fifteen planes carry FACE_RELIEF 2.0 so the planes survive framing distance, and the iris is a HUMAN iris (the blade's green emission read as a robot visor) - the bake and wrinkle caches re-versioned 90/93 to 107 on both runtimes so nothing stale wears. THE PRESENCE: the framing now SOLVES from the subject's MEASURED world box at each shot type's promised fill (ESTABLISHING 0.28 through EXTREME_CLOSEUP 1.15, the tight framings solving from the measured head) instead of a hardcoded 0.9m guess, with the evidence riding state.render.presence; and the LENS OWNS ITS SIGHTLINE - the solve steps the base angle aside first (12/24/36 degrees, framing preserved) then walks the distance in bounded, because the first triad night put the medium INSIDE a temple pillar (the vision read 'a white pillar and floor, no character visible') and the walk's own night taught its order (walking in first framed torso closeups and fog plates). TWO REAL FINDS rode home in the law books: the angle contract was broken end to end (_Framing stored radians into _solve_position's degree contract, silently flattening EVERY framed shot's camera to ~-1.2 degrees dead front-on - the -100-degree spread cancelled since iteration 88), and the -100 relic itself lands behind the shoulder at honest degrees, so the spread now aims at the face arc (+-24 degrees around dead front). THE SEAMS the crafted night queued are CLOSED: the BLENDER tick's bridge-lost degrade consults the job file's concluded state first (a done-with-clip job LANDS as REVIEW - the clipless-SIM residue is dead), the staleness tool and localJobStale read done/mp4Path before failing a RENDERING row (a finished-but-unticked render is never failed), and the asset builder's rig contract answers the DNA (a weapon-less figure owns no blade joint - Lin Yue's regex DNA had refused every library rebuild). THE ASSET LAW held: a law that changes the pixels is an asset change - the character asset key re-versioned 106 to 107 on both runtimes and all five cast assets rebuilt through the triad laws with vision inspections. THE PROOFS: blender-triad-smoke.py (30 direct checks + the render half: the palette ramp ends, the wash node, the stored-palette reuse, the cap's face band, the features measured proud of the ellipsoid, the relief + faceHash determinism, the measured subject, the fill solves, the sightline walk against a wall) and e2e-iter107-triad.ts (13 checks: the 107 keys mirror bit-exactly TS-py, the done-with-clip tick lands REVIEW, the named failure, the honest degrade, both staleness laws, the tool lands the unticked conclusion) - ALL GREEN. THE NIGHTS: cycle 1 framed pillars and fog plates (the walk order), cycle 2 shot the figure from behind the shoulder (the -100 relic), cycle 3 rides the face arc - the ledger median 3.5s/f (the triad renders FASTER than the crafted night's 9.09), no regressions, the cost line clean. THE RE-SCORE (real vision over the final cycle's clips): S002 0.05 ('a 3D rendered robotic figure'), S003 0.00 ('a dark, stylized 3D character' - though the close-up now frames pale eyeballs, brows, mouth and topknot dead-on: the mechanics read, the likeness does not), S004 0.00 (empty plates - the PAN swings into a pillar mid-move), S005 refused by the word-boundary law. THE VERDICT: BELOW over 5 readings (mean 2%, median 0%, p10 0%, floor 70%); the REAL /api/publish refused verbatim with every under cell named, the PUBLISH_RUN cadence SKIPPED, zero PUBLISH events - three cycles, three refusals, the gate's teeth never softened. THE FRONTIER, NAMED BY THE DISTRIBUTION: (1) THE BODY READ - the figure still reads robotic/blocky: the limb segments and joint balls are the mannequin anatomy the bevel law never reached (proportions and mass are the next sculpt law); (2) THE FACE'S LIGHT - the features exist and face the lens but the head reads dark-metallic under the night key: the hero key must aim at the face and the hairline sits at the brow; (3) THE MOVE VOCABULARY'S COLLISION AWARENESS - the base solve walks clear but a PAN's swing can still enter the world mid-move; (4) the sheet wash is subtle at wide. The next work order is the body read, in the light, with the moves collision-aware.

17. **THE STUDIO DOOR WORKS FROM THE PREVIEW FRAME (the direct session mint).** The member-facing sign-in had never once landed a session through the platform's preview: the edge proxies the app with a rewritten Host (localhost:3000), so Auth.js derived its base URL as `http://localhost:3000` and answered every successful credentials callback with `Location: http://localhost:3000/` - a host that exists on nobody's browser - and the member saw "Something went wrong" forever while the server log showed the session cookie actually being set on the same 302. THE FIX - no Auth.js internals forked, the standard flow stays intact for every E2E suite on localhost: (1) a DIRECT MINT route (`POST/DELETE /api/auth/login`) verifies email+password against the bcrypt row and mints the SAME session token Auth.js would - the JWE `encode` from next-auth/jwt, secret = the shared AUTH_SECRET, salt = the exact cookie name the read side derives (`authjs.session-token`; proxy.ts and sessionUser never pass secureCookie, so the name is deterministic) - and sets it on a JSON response: no redirect to chase, no CSRF cookie dance, no origin derivation to get wrong; the read side is untouched (getToken keeps decoding the same cookie, /api/auth/session keeps answering from it - verified live: session JSON returns the OWNER row through the real preview edge). (2) the sign-in page and the sign-out menu item drive the direct mint (full navigation after login so server components and the session provider remount against the fresh cookie). (3) `AUTH_URL`/`NEXTAUTH_URL` pinned to http://localhost:3000 in .env so cookie naming can never drift with edge headers, and `allowedDevOrigins` names the preview host so Next stops warning on every /_next subresource. (4) a platform env trap caught on the way: the container exports a default `DATABASE_URL` (a nonexistent path) that BEATS .env - the dev server must be started with the explicit `DATABASE_URL=file:.../AnimeOS/db/custom.db`, the same convention the E2E suites already follow. PROOFS through the real preview edge: login 200 + session cookie, /api/projects 200, /api/auth/session returns the OWNER session, wrong password 401, logout clears the cookie, signed-in `GET /` loads without the signin bounce; the localhost standard credentials flow still redirects and authenticates exactly as before; src tsc clean, eslint clean on the touched files.

18. **THE HULL-INK NIGHT BURNED FOR REAL - the distribution moved 11x and the gate held (the face is the frontier).** The re-score night ran over the standing production (EP07 Sc12, six shots) on the DESIGNED build (design crew v109 + the hull-ink law, the first production-level render of the 108/109/110 era on this machine): all six shots landed REAL Blender clips through the local pool, the real vision re-score judged pose-matched filmstrips against the model sheets, and the REAL /api/publish refused verbatim with every under cell named - the teeth never softened. THE NIGHT'S HONEST OPS (a re-learning of iteration 106's wall, twice): the batch fire of six overflowed the two-slot 3D pool and four shots fell through to MOTION/SIMULATOR stand-ins (the engine's own overflow law working as designed - the driver redid them); the restarted bridge's boot-resume then spawned THREE job workers past the pool law and the 4GB host OOM-killed a worker mid-render (the designed builds carry ~600MB RSS each); the pool's honest size on this host is ONE designed render at a time, the night driver drains serially, and the staleness tool landed two finished-but-unticked renders as REVIEW by reading the job files' own conclusions (done + mp4Path - the finalize never lies). THE DISTRIBUTION: mean 22% / median 20% / p10 10% over 3 scored shots (the prior nights: mean 2%, median 0%, p10 0%) - an 11x mean lift, the first readings where the scorer says the pixels resemble the DESIGN: S002 35%/45% ("retains the general silhouette and color palette... the teal/blue gradient of the robes more closely resemble Clone 001" - hair 40-50%, wardrobe 40-60%, palette 40-50%), S003 10% ("the face is obscured by a mask, the wardrobe is entirely different... 3D chibi style"), S004 20% ("the silhouette and long hair match, but the wardrobe is simplified to a plain white robe, the face lacks detail"); S001/S005/S006 REFUSED by the anchor law (no featured character with a model sheet). THE GATE: "EP07's identity distribution sits BELOW the release floor: mean 24%, median 20%, p10 10%, floor 70% - the under cells: calm, close, locked, shadow, all worst at E7 Sc12 S003. Repair the distribution, then stage. An episode does not publish BELOW the floor." THE FRONTIER, NAMED BY TONIGHT'S DISTRIBUTION: the body/palette read CLEARED into resemblance territory (the design crew's spec-driven builds + hull ink work) - what sinks the scores now is (1) THE FACE (10-20% across every scored shot: the rendered face does not carry the sheet's face at framing distance), (2) the STYLE read ("simplified geometry", "3D chibi" - the mannequin anatomy again), (3) wardrobe fidelity drift on S003/S004 (plain robes where the sheet carries layers/accent). The next work order stands: the mannequin-anatomy law + face key light, aimed at the face cells. Tools this night leaves behind: scripts/night111-run.ts (the serial drain driver: junk-driver redo scope, POOL admission, the direct-mint session, the UI-equivalent tick) and scripts/night111-rescore.ts (the real-vision re-score + the distribution + the verbatim publish attempt).



Each item lands the same way the last eighteen iterations did: one thin, testable slice at a time, every step verified against the production database and the running studio.

19. **THE FACE CARRIES THE SHEET (the asset-law bump aimed at the face cells) - and the loop learned to look at its own pixels.** The 112 rescore named the frontier: the face cells at 10-20%, the "3D chibi"/mannequin style read, the hairline. The 113 laws: (1) THE MANNEQUIN BREAKS - the body graph carries calf/thigh/deltoid/forearm profiles and a torso S-curve (the v3.x joint anchors untouched); (2) THE HAIRLINE SITS ABOVE THE BROWS (theta 0.62 -> 1.02) and the hair radii + face pivots ride headScale (a 0.9 skull no longer floats under 1.0-scale features); (3) THE EYES CARRY THE FACE - the painted decals grew to 0.070/0.072 and THE IRIS IS DARK (a dark mass with a bottom glint + a thicker lash - the old bright-bottom gradient read as a pale ring at production distance); (4) THE FACE KEEPS ITS LIGHT - a shadowless face fill rides the hero key and both track a FaceTarget empty on the head's front; (5) THE LEVEL LENS - tight framings ride the face's own height and the choreo dolly scales with the framing distance (the absolute lean was a face-plant on a 0.5m closeup). THE EYE TEST caught four render-path bugs no score had named: the hull ink shells FLEW (matrix_world assigned before parent = the parent transform squared - a knot of ink floating above every figure since the hull law landed), the head's hide_render normal proxy grew an ink hull and closed a black shell over the face, the second figure's stand-off placement was wiped by apply_pose (the clone stood INSIDE the hero), and the pool worker's stub purge let every job render through the previous jobs' accumulated scene. THE CEL LAW, STATED AND KEPT: the lights pick the band, never the value - the tree is now a flat two-tone emission mix (lit = the dye, shadow = the cooled floor, the toon node only measures the band) because the old lit-band-plus-floor sum clipped pale skin to a blown white egg at close range. THE DISTRIBUTION (EP07 Sc12, 6/6 real Blender clips, real vision, the REAL gate): S002 35% (face cells 10-20 -> 40), S004 50% (hair 60/wardrobe 70/palette 80), S003 5% ("a different gender and species" - the closeup, the one frontier left), 3 anchor refusals; MEAN 30% / MEDIAN 35% / P10 5% (112 read 22/20/10) - and the publish gate refused verbatim with every under cell named. THE FRONTIER IS EXACTLY ONE SHOT: the ground-truth harness renders the same builder + the same payload DNA in a cold process and the face reads beautifully - the production closeup's breakage lives in the shot's integration (the temple set's green-dominant walls/lights + the wash + the comp at 0.5m), which is the 114 work order, alongside the anchor-law front (S001/S005/S006 need featured sheets before the gate can read them). THE OPERATIONAL LAW the night burned in: a resident restart is part of every law change (the pool worker serves stale modules until restarted), and a pooled worker that keeps its warmth must also keep its hygiene (the purge).

20. **THE CLOSEUP STAGES ITS OWN SET (the shot-side integration the 113 evidence ordered) - and the anchor front cleared.** The 113 night localized the closeup's breakage to the SHOT's integration, not the asset; the 114 laws answer it: (1) THE CLOSEUP OWNS DEPTH - tight framings ride real portrait glass (DOF f/2.0, focus TRACKED TO THE FACE PLANE via the FaceTarget empty, so the pull survives every pose and move; ECU 1.8, MCU 2.8) and a tight PREVIEW renders 24 samples for the aperture, wide framings keep the 10-sample fast path; (2) THE SET STAGES FOR THE TIGHT LENS - every material present when the set finishes building is tagged set-owned (the cast builds after, so its dye can never ride the tag; the pool purge removes the blocks between jobs), and at tight framings those surfaces pull desaturated and darker (CLOSEUP sat 0.55/val 0.62 - hue untouched: the temple stays a temple, just one the eye can leave), before the cel conversion so the dye inherits the staged tone - bounded, deterministic, per-shot, never AI-painted; (3) THE ANCHOR FRONT - S001/S005/S006's descriptions now NAME their featured cast (the same detectCast feeds the engine's figures AND the identity gate, so pixels and judge read the same cast), and Lin Yue's committed r2 design DNA was wired byte-exact (the crew NOT re-run: the 114 night changes the SHOT side, never the asset). THE STUDIO UI REVIEW CAME FIRST: login holds, the six shots read in REVIEW, the queue shows LIVE BLENDER + real durations, and the eye test on the clips' own frames saw S003 transformed - the level face, the dark irises, the sawtooth hairline, and the temple receded into a soft tone behind the eyes. THE DISTRIBUTION (EP07 Sc12, 6/6 real clips, real vision, the REAL gate): S001 35% (was REFUSED), S002 50% (was 35), S003 45% (was 5 - the frontier broke: style 90%, face 60%, "the facial structure and anime style are consistent with the model sheet"), S004 35% (was 50, vision variance on an untouched dark wide), S005 50% (was REFUSED), S006 10% (was REFUSED - Lin Yue 65%, the night's best, but Demon Lord Wei 10%: no designed build, a stand-off silhouette in a lightning wash); MEAN 38% / MEDIAN 40% / P10 10% (113: 30/35/5), scored 6 refused 0 (113: 3 refused) - and the publish gate refused verbatim with every under cell named. THE NEXT FRONTIER IS NAMED BY THE NIGHT ITSELF: the ANTAGONIST (the design crew for Demon Lord Wei), the hair tint under the level lens (black reading teal-green at 0.5m), and the wide-framing style law. Tools: night114-restore.ts (the committed-DNA restore), detached-night114.mjs + detached-rescore114.mjs (the detach law: the tool session reaps children).
21. **THE ANTAGONIST RIDES THE CREW - and three dye/staging seams died under the night's own lights.** The 114 night named its frontier; the 115 night answers all three cells: (1) THE ANTAGONIST - Demon Lord Wei took the designed path (the SHEET READER read his anchored sheet, 2 build/judge/tune rounds, best 25% vs the 72% bar - below it, honestly): the landed spec rides Character.designSpec and every future render builds him (S004's aura wide + S006's stand-off now build a DESIGNED antagonist) - and the TUNER's one mistake was caught by the director's eye: it confused the sheet's teal hanfu with his HAIR (#5f9ea0); the sheet is canon, the spec field was corrected to the sheet's own black (#0a0a0a). (2) THE HAIR TINT, three seams deep - the 114 "black hair reading teal-green at 0.5m" was never one bug: SEAM A, the hair sheen was a REAL glossy BSDF whose band carried the SCENE's light colors (the pale-blue moon sun multiplying the temple's own emissive cel walls) - THE CEL LAW kept to its letter: the glossy toon survives only as the band MEASURER and the paint is a dye-derived emission (the moon decides WHERE the glint sits, the sheet decides WHAT COLOR it is); SEAM B, the groom's curve material never carried a dye tag, so the cel conversion dyed the strands generic grey (the tag routes them through the hair path with the sheet's own hex); SEAM C - the deep one, three burns to root-cause - Lin Yue's sheet palette measured ALL-PALE/TEAL clusters (the extractor's dominant-bucket read missed the dark hair region entirely), so the near-black hair dye found its "nearest cluster" at mid-teal and the sheet-DNA pull (0.75!) landed ~#516b6a: the closeup's hair rendered PALE TEAL. The law that killed it: THE PULL NEVER CROSSES THE VALUE CLASS - when no sheet cluster shares the dye's value class (|dLum| > 0.15), the palette cannot see that dye and the pull REFUSES; the sheet DNA's own measured read stands un-pulled (a capped blend was tried first and still injected the wrong HUE - the third burn's muted slate-teal proved the refusal is the honest form). And the sheen measurer itself SATURATES on a smooth hair dome under several large lights - THE SHEEN IS A GLINT: the band crosses a hard cel edge (0.55) and lives only where the specular response is strong. (3) THE WIDE KEEPS ITS AIR - the depth mist now stages per framing (ESTABLISHING 7.0/26.0/0.72, WIDE 5.5/16.0/0.75 - start/depth/intensity) for atmospheric perspective; the FIRST BURN taught the shape of the law: a short depth fogged the far field into a WALL of flat tint (S001's review read "an empty void, crushed blacks, no depth layering" at 0.462) - the air is a LONG GRADIENT with a CAPPED intensity, never a fog-out, and the 112 ghost law holds (the staged starts sit past the figure's own framing distance). THE NIGHT RAN FOUR BURNS, each caught by its own evidence (the render judge's review, the vision re-score's hair cells, the eye test on the frames): void -> gradient; pale-teal dye -> value guard; saturated sheen -> glint threshold; and the fourth burn BROKE THE CLOSEUP FRONTIER PROPERLY: S003 Lin Yue 65% (face 70, hair 60, palette 70, style 60 - was 45% at its 114 best and 15-20 mid-burns; the eye test confirms: neutral black mass, sawtooth hairline, strands with air, dark irises, the temple receded), S002 60% (hair 70, palette 80), S006 Lin Yue 65% (joint-best); the GATE: "EP07's identity distribution sits BELOW the release floor: mean 49%, median 50%, p10 35%, worst 20% over 12 reading(s), floor 70%. The under cells: locked, storm, wide, calm - all worst at E7 Sc12 S006" - the gate held verbatim (114: 41/45/20/10), the p10 nearly doubled, and THE WORST CELL MOVED: S003 -> S006, whose 20% is a 20px stand-off silhouette at 28mm - geometry-capped, a blocking/camera law, not a dye law. THE HONEST OPS: S002 fell through to a MOTION stand-in mid-night (the driver's admission count raced the pool worker's finalize window) and the night REFUSED to bless 5/6 ("NOT every shot rides a real clip - the re-score would judge stand-ins") until a second drain pass landed the real Blender clip; the resident restart law was re-burned twice (the dev server's keeper respawns the bridge, and a hand restart needs ANIMEOS_BLENDER_BIN in its env). THE NEXT FRONTIER IS NAMED: the antagonist's wide readability (the 28mm two-shot's blocking, Wei's wardrobe/embroidery at distance) and the palette extractor's dark-region blindness (the value guard is the guardrail; the extractor should SEE dark clusters). PROOFS: blender-anime-smoke ALL GREEN (the v113 assertions hold), blender-toon-smoke ALL GREEN (the hull law, the face-tracked key, the staged framing, wash nodes retuned), e2e-iter109 ALL GREEN, src tsc 0 errors, eslint clean on the touched files. Tools: scripts/night115-recon.ts (the standing scene's shots + cast state), scripts/night115-crew-wei.ts (the antagonist's crew run with the landed-spec proof), scripts/detached-night115.mjs (crew|drain|rescore phases - the detach law), scripts/detached-bridge-restart.mjs (the resident restart with the full law env).
22. **THE TWO-SHOT BLOCKS BOTH - and the palette learned to see the dark.** The 115 night named its frontier; the 116 night answers both cells and the evidence caught two deeper seams mid-night: (1) THE TWO-SHOT BLOCKS BOTH - the 28mm stand-off's second figure stood at a HARDCODED (0.6, 1.7) behind-right of the hero while the camera solved from the hero's box alone, so Wei rendered a dark-on-dark smudge at the frame's right edge; the blocking is now a LAW OF THE LENS: the second figure of a wide two-shot (WS/WIDE/ESTABLISHING/LOW_ANGLE) crosses to the lens-perpendicular through the hero (both figures read in profile, the classic duel staging), the gap is the framing's stand-off distance scaled by the measured hero, and the figure FACES the hero (rot from atan2, not a constant); the tight framings keep the 113 stand-off exactly (the closeup's hair-curtain lesson holds). (2) THE PAIR IS THE SUBJECT - the framing context now solves the wide two-shot from the UNION of both figures' measured boxes: the aim lands the pair's midpoint chest, the distance also solves the WIDTH across the lens (the horizontal 18mm half-angle; the height solve alone framed the hero and left the antagonist at the edge), and the sightline walk orbits the pair's own origin; the face lights keep the HERO's box (the pair's headH would mis-scale the key). (3) THE STAND-OFF READS - a dark wardrobe on a night set is a silhouette without an edge: the second figure of a wide two-shot gets a dedicated moon-tinted rim (#cfd8e8, 0.35 of the key's energy, 155 degrees off the to-camera vector - behind, past the profile edge) whose TOON band draws the cel edge the wardrobe's own value cannot give against a storm wall. (4) THE EXTRACTOR SEES THE DARK - the dominant-bucket census is a brightness lie (paper and skin own the pixel count; Lin Yue's black hair measured all-pale/teal through 115 and the value guard could only refuse): the stratified pass now gives every value class that owns >= 5% of the sheet its own dominant cluster (>= 12% of the class, same separation discipline, dark first, at most two join) - Lin Yue's palette carries his black #16161a and the hair/boots rows flipped from REFUSED to honestly "already true". (5) THE SKIP EARNS ITS SKIP - "already true to the sheet" must never forgive a VALUE drift: Wei's robe DNA (#5b7d7e mid-teal) stood 0.090 from the sheet's own dark-teal cluster (#476565) - inside SKIP_BELOW - but the distance WAS a value step (dLum 0.091) and the storm wash read the robe pale; the skip now holds only when the nearest cluster also shares the dye's value (SKIP_LUM_CAP 0.05), and Wei's robe APPLIED #5b7d7e -> #4c6b6b, the sheet's own dark teal. THE EVIDENCE CAUGHT TWO DEEPER SEAMS MID-NIGHT: the pair framing never engaged on the first burn because measure_subject matched the head mesh by EXACT name - a scene that already owns "HeadMesh" names the second build's head "HeadMesh.001", so EVERY second figure measured None (the antagonist did not exist to the presence law; the match is a name-FAMILY match now) - and the sheet conformance application ran BEFORE the second figure's build, so every B row skipped "no such material on the stage" and the antagonist's sheet pull NEVER LANDED through 114 and 115 (the loop moved below both builds - THE SHEET DRESSES BOTH, AFTER BOTH BUILT). THE NIGHT RAN TWO BURNS and the re-render's evidence is the law's own proof: twoShot engaged (pair span 1.85x0.88, midpoint aim, dist 3.224), RobeMatB applied the dark teal, the blocking evidence names loc/rot/gap/facesHero, the rim names yaw/rise/energy - and the frame itself: Lin Yue on the left third, Wei on the right third, both in profile facing each other, the dark teal reading against the storm wall with the moon rim on his edge (the 115 frame had one pale figure and an invisible smudge). THE SCORES: S006's shot worst 20% -> 30% (Wei 20 -> 30, his WARDROBE cell 10 -> 40, palette 50-75) - the targeted cells moved; but the distribution read mean 37% / median 38% / p10 20% / worst 15% and THE GATE REFUSED VERBATIM - and the honest finding is VARIANCE: S001/S002/S005's pixels are IDENTICAL to 115's (the conformance rows are provably the same bytes) yet the re-score re-rolled them -20 to -25 per cell (115 named it untouched-path variance; at establishing scale the figure is ~20px and the face/style cells are noise). THE NEXT FRONTIER IS NAMED: S006's DIRECTED ACTION (the clip stages the stand-off but the pixel review demands the clash, the lightning and the debris - "Lightning detonation" in the seed's lighting note never compiles into lightning DNA and the shot carries no clash grammar/poses/fx: the stand-off is blocked, nothing HAPPENS yet), the ESTABLISHING readability (S001's tiny-figure cells) and the re-score's per-cell variance (multi-sample stabilization), and Wei's face/embroidery at distance (the vision's remaining wardrobe note). PROOFS: blender-toon-smoke ALL GREEN, blender-anime-smoke ALL GREEN, blender-camera-smoke ALL GREEN, e2e-iter109 ALL GREEN, src tsc 0 errors, eslint clean on the touched files; the two-shot probe (scripts/probe-two-shot-116.py) measures BOTH rigs and proves the placement math (loc (1.48, 0.60), rot -68 deg, facing exactly at the hero). Tools: scripts/detached-night116.mjs (drain|rescore), scripts/probe-two-shot-116.py (the second-figure measure probe).


23. **THE EXPERT CREW STANDS UP - the choreographer directs the clash, and the verdict learned to be a median.** The question that named this iteration: anime and donghua are made by ARTISTS - so why not agent experts per craft that DSH delegates to? THE ANSWER IS THE CREW, WITH THE LAW A HUNDRED ITERATIONS EARNED: an agent is THE EYE AND THE PEN, NEVER THE HAND - each expert owns its craft as LAWS (deterministic compilers that produce the pixels, auditable and free at render time) and as JUDGE RUBRICS (the vision readings that name which law moved wrong); the knowledge lives in the REPO (skill briefs, law libraries, sheets, the ledger), never in model memory. `src/lib/crew/experts.ts` is the honest roster - nine benches, every STANDING one naming the laws that make it real (character-designer: the 109 crew; cinematographer: the 113-116 blocking/framing/DOF laws; colorist: the value-stratified extractor; art-director: toon bands + hull ink + the stand-off rim; sculptor, groomer, lighter: their passes; choreographer: THIS iteration) and one PLANNED (the storyteller, first law pending). THE FIRST EXPERT LANDS WORKING - THE ACTION CHOREOGRAPHER (`src/lib/crew/action-choreographer.ts`, pure + deterministic): a shot's own directed text (description + lighting + audio cues) compiles into ACTION DNA in the worker's exact vocabulary - the clash phrase LUNGE->SLASH->BLOCK->STANCE as grammar beats whose boundaries ARE the shot's audio-cue times (S006's "Impact detonation" at 200ms and "Debris scatter" at 1100ms become the strike's end and the lock's end, so the sound and the picture land on one clock), the BURST bound to the beat whose entry IS the impact, DEBRIS bound to the scatter's beat, REACTION the body owes the violence, TRAIL only when the text names a blade (the expert invents nothing), AURA for gathered energy, a two-beat flash clock for storm-without-clash - and THE DECLINE LAW: text that names no action (S001's establishing crane, S003's closeup, S002's entrance) is declined HONESTLY, silence stays silent, pixels stay stable. THE CONSULT (`choreographer-consult.ts`, hooked in createRenderJob so every queue path funnels through it) fills ONLY EMPTY columns, per column - EXPLICIT DIRECTION OUTRANKS THE EXPERT - lands the event naming what it directed, and returns its patch so the caller's own row assembles THIS job's payload from the directed columns (the first burn's evidence caught the bug: the consult patched a copy, the payload read the original - grammarBeats 0 on the wire while the columns sat SET; the patch contract killed it). THE RANK LAW IS TESTED (the e2e's fillIfEmpty mirrors the consult's has(): a directed column is a non-empty JSON array - a corrupt column reads empty). ONE LAW, TWO RUNTIMES, PROVEN BOTH WAYS: the e2e feeds the expert's grammar through normalizeGrammar (the TS wire mirror) AND through fx_pass.normalize_fx / physics_pass.normalize_physics (the worker's own bpy-free validators) - the burst's beat-1 binding and the debris' beat-2 binding survive the worker's clock. THE NIGHT BURNED TWICE (6/6 real Blender clips): the evidence is the law's own proof - S006: grammarBeats 4, BURST burstsFired 1 (maxRing 3.15) at beat 1, DEBRIS strikes 1 / bounces 1 / maxSpeed 5.02 / settleFrame 63, REACTION reactions 1 / maxOffset 0.062 / recoverFrame 20; S004: flash burst fired (maxRing 3.05) + AURA; S005: TRAIL trailPeak 9.3 + AURA - and the frames: the blade UP in the guard, the white shockwave ring expanding with shards flying, rubble lying where it settled between the duellists (116's statues now perform their first clash). THE VERDICT IS THE MEDIAN OF ITS SAMPLES (`scoreShotIdentityMedian`): 116's finding was identical pixels re-rolling -20/-25 between passes - the same artifact now goes to the vision channel 3 times (ANIMEOS_SCORE_SAMPLES) and the per-entry MEDIAN persists through the exact single-verdict tail (one row shape, steadier truth); the night's numbers: first pass p10 10% -> final pass p10 25% (S006's worst cell 10 -> 25, Wei lit out of the dark by the burst), mean 38 / median 38, scored 6 refused 0, the gate refused VERBATIM with every under cell named - the floor is still far and the frontier is honestly named. THE UI REVIEW (login holds, LIVE BLENDER healthy, the queue card carries the BURST/DEBRIS chips) named THE NEXT SEAM ITSELF: the DSH inspection reviews ONE poster frame - "Frame captures zero action... directed FX (BURST@1) and physics (DEBRIS) are entirely absent from this frame" - while the eye test at the impact beat shows the ring, the shards and the rubble plainly: THE ACTION LIVES ACROSS FRAMES AND THE INSPECTOR IS BLIND TO TIME. THE STEER, IN ORDER: (1) the inspection artifact must be a FILMSTRIP sampled at the shot's own cue times (the strip builder exists - the identity scorer already obeys this law); (2) the SECOND FIGURE never answers the clash (the hero performs, Wei holds stance - the paired performance law: the hero's SLASH is Wei's BLOCK); (3) identity at action scale (the established frontier: Wei's face/embroidery at distance, establishing-scale reads). PROOFS: smoke-choreographer 37 checks ALL GREEN (S006's exact brief compiles beat-for-beat to the cue times; declines honest; determinism byte-for-byte), e2e-iter117 22 checks ALL GREEN (rank law, TS mirror, Python mirror), blender-toon/anime/camera smokes ALL GREEN, src tsc 0 errors, eslint clean on the touched files. 24. **THE FORGE OPENS - every built-in addon for the whole crew, the canonical poses become Blender-native assets, and the mannequin gains a body.** The studio's directive: DSH and the expert crew get access to ALL of Blender's built-in addons, because a wanglin-level character (not a stand-in, not a mannequin) is built with Blender's whole toolbox - so USE the full power, not just hold it. THREE LAWS LANDED. (1) THE ADDON REGISTRY LAW (`bridges/blender/animeos_addons.py`): every worker session opens the forge - every bundled addon enabled before the first scene build (policy env ANIMEOS_ADDONS: "all" default | "off" | csv), idempotent (~5 ms warm; 104 ms cold), because the pool's `read_factory_settings` purge re-closes the non-defaults after EVERY job (the probe caught it: only 8 of 13 survive the reset) so the re-ensure is the top of every `worker_run` and the pool boot; THE CORE LAW is honest - Blender 5.2 loads rigify from scripts/addons_core OUTSIDE addon_utils' bookkeeping (check() says disabled while every class is registered), so an enable whose module still imports is AVAILABLE (core), recorded, never a failure; the summary rides the job state (`addons` + `addonsLine`) - the night's real renders each carry "forge: 13 built-ins open, 0 failed, policy all (104ms)". (2) THE CREW CATALOG (`src/lib/crew/experts.ts`): BLENDER_FORGE - all 13 built-ins named, each with the bench that owns it and what it is FOR in THIS studio (rigify -> the sculptor's door to production rigs; pose_library + io_anim_bvh -> the choreographer; cycles + node_wrangler + bl_pkg + hydra_storm -> the art-director; the io_* interchange -> the designer; svg/translate -> the storyteller; vr -> the cinematographer), every bench's `addons` ownership on the roster, `forgeContextLine()` for DSH - and the law that keeps: ACCESS IS NOT LICENSE, enabling a tool grants the TOOL, the pixels still come only from laws that earned their keep through a night (the colorist/groomer/lighter honestly own none - their crafts ride core Blender: hair curves, cloth/flesh solvers, light rigs). (3) THE POSE LIBRARY LAW (`bridges/blender/animeos_pose_library.py`): the canonical grammar vocabulary (13 POSE_JOINTS poses, STANCE through POINT) built ONCE as real Blender Pose Assets on an armature whose bone names are the production rig's (_bone_table convention), transformed with the SAME convention apply_pose drives every frame (spine/head +X, shoulders +X, elbows/hips -X, the knees taking the leg-IK solve's fold-only answer), saved to `public/pose-library/animeos_poses_v1.blend` and re-verified by reopening the file - the tandem crew shares ONE Blender-native vocabulary a future linker can retarget onto any studio figure. PLUS THE ANATOMY LAW (`bridges/blender/body_anatomy.py`) - the mannequin gains a body: 22 analytic 3-D Gaussian structures (pectoral planes + sternal groove, deltoid caps, trapezius, lats, scapular flats, abdominal ridge, quadriceps, knee caps, calf bellies, shin lines, forearm ridges) displace the baked skin along its own vertex normals BEFORE binding and BEFORE the garment face-kill - deterministic, bit-exact across rebuilds (the seed law holds: identical evidence JSON twice), smooth by design (the Laplacian does not move: form, not noise), and THE SCULPTOR SCALES THE TOOL WITH THE BODY: every center offset and sigma rides the layout factors the body graph itself uses (w/sw/hp) with the spec's build and gender driving the amplitudes - a female lean sculpts softer, a heavy male stronger (both proven by differential smoke), and the spec reach was caught honestly: the first smoke fed `body` at DNA top level where resolve_spec only reads designSpec.body - identical numbers across three specs named the seam before the night could lie. THE NIGHT: 6/6 real Blender clips through the restarted resident (a resident restart IS part of every law change - the old pool worker predates the forge); the evidence on the wire (forge open, all 22 field hits on the real builds); the re-score (median of 3) moved the distribution mean 38 -> 45, median 38 -> 40, p10 25 -> 30, and S003's worst cell HIT THE FLOOR at 70% (was 55) - S001 +10 (35), S002 +10 (55), S004 +10 (45), S006 +5 (30), S005 -5 (35); the gate refused VERBATIM (BELOW, every under cell named: worst at E7 Sc12 S006) - an episode does not publish BELOW the floor. THE EYE TEST: the closeup holds its clean read (level eyes, dark irises, sawtooth hairline with air), the two-shot stages the duel with the debris chunk lying between the profiles, the fitted silhouette reads a waist - the anatomy's form is present but SUBTLE at establishing scale (~20px figures), which is the honest frontier. THE STEER, IN ORDER: (1) the anatomy v2 - SILHOUETTE-driven form (the read at establishing scale is the score's worst cell: the body must lie less at 20px), (2) the paired performance law (the hero's SLASH is Wei's BLOCK - the second figure still never answers the clash; the pose library is built for exactly this), (3) the inspection filmstrip at the shot's own cue times (the inspector still reads ONE poster frame and calls a performing clip static), (4) the forge evidence surfaced in the UI (the state carries it; the queue card does not show it yet). PROOFS: blender-addons-smoke 14 checks ALL GREEN (13/13 open, rigify honestly core, re-ensure idempotent + cheap, purge recovery, policies honored, 13 pose assets verified round-trip), blender-anatomy-smoke 11 checks ALL GREEN (22 structures land, bit-exact, spec-driven amplitudes, full figure builds + rigs over the sculpted body), blender-toon/anime/camera smokes ALL GREEN (the anime smoke now asserts THE FORGE OPENS on the worker + the anatomy evidence rides the state), e2e-iter118-forge ALL GREEN (catalog integrity, TS/Python name parity, bench ownership, the pose library file real on disk), e2e-iter109 + e2e-iter117 ALL GREEN, smoke-choreographer GREEN, src tsc 0 errors, eslint clean. Tools: scripts/blender-addons-smoke.py, scripts/blender-anatomy-smoke.py, scripts/e2e-iter118-forge.ts, scripts/detached-night118.mjs (drain|rescore).

25. **THE DUEL PERFORMS - the paired performance law, the silhouette leads, and the inspection sees the performance.** The 118 steer executed in order, on the named frontier. (1) THE PAIRED PERFORMANCE LAW (`src/lib/animation/paired-performance.ts`, `Shot.pairedChoreo`): a two-character shot is ONE performance written on two bodies - the hero's SLASH is Wei's BLOCK. THE ANSWER TABLE is fixed craft knowledge (a SLASH is met by a BLOCK with a CROUCH recoil; a LUNGE gives ground to a FALL; a LEAP by a CROUCH that rises), `compilePairedProgram` derives the partner's keyed program on ONE CLOCK (the hero's strike moments are kept exactly; the guard forms in the wind-up, meets the strike ON its key, holds through the read, takes the recoil between the read and the recover, and recovers by the last frame) and carries NO impact and NO smear of its own - ONE CLASH, ONE LIGHT (the hero program's impact flare is the clash's only light). `set_shot_choreography` derives it automatically on a two-cast strike program (an explicit `pairedChoreo` argument wins; clearing the choreo clears the answer with it), and the CHOREOGRAPHER'S CONSULT (iteration 117's submit-time expert) now also derives it from the directed grammar's pose beats when the shot's cast detects a partner - the derivation is the same answer table, one implementation, two entry points. THE BRIDGE PERFORMS IT: `compose_standoff` re-composes the 113 stand-off mark on top of every per-frame pose offset ROTATED with the partner's own facing (a recoil steps BACK from the hero, never sideways through the set; a STANCE pose composes back to the exact mark - the 113 blocking law is a fixed point), then the rig syncs - the second figure performs every frame where it held one static STANCE through 118. The directive canon rides the pairing (SHOT_DIRECTIVE_VERSION 98 -> 119, `|paired=|v2`, both runtimes mirrored). (2) THE SILHOUETTE LEADS (`body_anatomy.py` v2): the 118 forms were present but read ~1px at establishing scale - v2 splits the field into SILHOUETTE-class structures (delts, traps, lats, pecs, quads, calves - the outline a wide shot reads) at 2.2x gain and SURFACE-class structures that keep the 118 voice, adds two OBLIQUE fields that CARVE the waist (negative displacement along the outward normals - the taper is carved, not added), and the evidence carries THE SILHOUETTE READ: the mesh's width at five landmark heights before vs after, in meters and as a fraction of stature, with the waist measured INSIDE the arms' shadow (the hanging arms would own the raw band width and the taper would never read). ANIME_LAW_VERSION 118 -> 119. (3) THE INSPECTION SEES THE PERFORMANCE (`render-review.ts` + `cueSampleTimestamps`): the vision layer judges a FILMSTRIP pulled at the shot's DIRECTED CUE TIMES (the choreography's keys and impact, the grammar beats' starts - a keyed performance is judged at the beats it performs, not at one arbitrary poster moment), capped at four frames, deduped, clamped, degrading honestly to the pose-sample strip and then the single poster with the source named; the pixel evidence tells the inspection exactly what it saw. (4) THE EVIDENCE LANDS ON THE CARDS: the forge one-liner rides telemetry as a FORGE span and the paired performance line rides the stage at landing - the queue card shows the FORGE engine line, and a rose DUEL chip flies on every paired shot. THE NIGHT: the pairings landed on S005 (the draw answered by a 4-key block program) and S006 (the clash answered by a 6-key block program, Wei named on the wire), 6/6 real Blender clips (S005's first take honestly degraded to SIM under the single-slot contention and was re-drained - the redo scope caught it), the paired evidence on the state (`pairedPerformance` + `pairedLine`), forge open on every build. THE EYE TEST: S006's clash READS - the hero lunges inside the burst ring while Wei's guard arm is UP meeting it (f0), the lock holds (f1), the break and settle relax both bodies (f2-f4) - the duel performs where 118 held a statue. The pixel reviews judged S005/S006 on cue filmstrips (2 and 3 frames at the directed beats). THE DISTRIBUTION (median of 3): mean 40, median 35, p10 30 - BELOW the 70% floor and the gate refused VERBATIM (the recurring honest note: wardrobe simplified, style low-poly vs the 2D sheets - the identity gap concentrates in wardrobe + style, now the named frontier). THE STEER, IN ORDER: (1) wardrobe/weapon craft on the DESIGNED figures (the identity gap's home), (2) the partner's REACTION - the paired body takes the physics stagger the hero's REACTION law already gives the hero, (3) the cue labels ride the rescore's filmstrip prompt (the vision model counted "frame 5 of 3" - tell it what each frame is), (4) the Wang Lin-grade face: the sculpts are on the body; the FACE is the next mountain. PROOFS: blender-paired-smoke 9 checks ALL GREEN (the answer compiles under the worker's law, one clock, one light, the guard READS >30 deg off stance, the recoil dips, the stand-off mark is the STANCE fixed point, bit-exact twice), blender-anatomy-smoke 15 checks ALL GREEN (24 structures, shoulders widen, the waist CARVES IN, smooth, bit-exact, spec-driven), blender-directive/anime/camera/addons/toon smokes ALL GREEN (the anime smoke asserts v119 on the worker), e2e-iter119-duel 56 checks ALL GREEN (the answer table covers the vocabulary, The Combo/Rising Fang/Draw Storm all derive compile-clean answers, determinism bit-exact, the cue law, the v119 canon both runtimes, every wiring pin), e2e-iter118-forge + e2e-iter117 + smoke-choreographer ALL GREEN, src tsc 0 errors, eslint clean. Tools: scripts/blender-paired-smoke.py, scripts/e2e-iter119-duel.ts, scripts/night119-pair.ts, scripts/detached-night119.mjs (drain|rescore).
26. **THE CHARACTER GAINS ITS CRAFT - the wardrobe crafts itself, the face gains structure, the partner takes the hit, and the strip says which frame it is.** The 119 steer executed in order, on the named frontier. (1) THE WARDROBE CRAFTS ITSELF (`anime_character.py`): the identity gap's home for three nights ("wardrobe simplified") answered with craft, not color - THE SKIRT GAINS FOLDS (below the waist the robe tube is modulated by two deterministic radial harmonics, 6th + 9th, that deepen toward the hem with a drifting phase - the vertical cloth folds a skirt owns when cloth hangs from a sash; pure analytic, bit-exact, the evidence carries the folded count + the max modulation fraction), THE HEM WEARS ITS MEASURED TRIM (a contrast yuanbian band lofted at the robe's own measured hem extent - measured AT the hem ring itself, because the loft's rings sit ~0.14 apart and a taller band above the hem owns no verts - the first build's evidence caught the empty band before the night could), THE CUFFS WEAR THEIR BANDS, THE CHEST WEARS ITS EMBROIDERY (a painted cloud-scroll yunwen band - silver thread, paired spiral curls alternating flip, border lines - conformed onto the robe chest by a shrinkwrap decal parented to the SPINE so it poses with the chest, the same paint law the face rides), and THE BLADE EARNS ITS FORGE (the sword ships three grip wrap bands, a pommel disc and a two-strand tassel; the staff ships hand wraps and end caps - every piece riding the 97 grip contract's single placement function `grip_piece_offset`). (2) THE FACE GAINS STRUCTURE (`bridges/blender/face_anatomy.py`, FACE_LAW_VERSION face-v1): the mountain's first ledge - 16 analytic Gaussian structures sculpt the skull (brow ridge, eye sockets, the nose bridge and tip the profile line reads, cheekbones, two lips, philtrum, chin, jaw edges, temples) displacing along OUTWARD-oriented normals - and the probe caught a skull truth the render never noticed: THE HEAD'S STORED NORMALS POINT INWARD (the from_pydata ring winding; the shading rides the sphere proxy's transferred normals so six iterations of toon reads never lied, but the sculpt needed the centroid-radial flip). THE TERMINATOR LAW IS UNTOUCHED - the sculpt moves GEOMETRY, the sphere-transferred shading normals stay, the face reads through its profile and outline the way anime faces do; THE SPEC DRIVES THE CARVE (a male face carries a 1.5x brow and a harder jaw, a female face higher cheeks and softer lips; the angular shape carves, the round shape lifts; chinFwd and jawTaper scale chin and jaw), and the evidence carries THE PROFILE READ - how proud of the face plane the brow, the nose bridge, the nose tip and the chin stand, in millimeters (the duel is fought in profile: the nose bridge IS the silhouette there). ANIME_LAW_VERSION 119 -> 120; the face anatomy evidence rides the anime dict beside the body's. (3) THE PARTNER TAKES THE HIT (`physics_pass.py` v12.1 + the bridge): a paired performance is ONE violence on TWO bodies - the debris answered the hero's law alone while the blocked partner stood like stone. `build_physics_rig` grew a PARTNER mode (kinds_filter compiles REACTION ONLY - the solid world's meshes belong to the hero rig, a second rubble ring would be a lie about one blast; strike_src reads the HERO rig's same-beat strikes through a beat TAG so a later beat never reads stale violence - the first build cleared the strikes before the partner could read them and the smoke caught it; strike_from names the striker's own mark - a blocked blade lives where the striker stands). The bridge synthesizes the partner's REACTION program bound at the PAIRED program's STRIKE beats (mapped through the grammar's beat clock, intensity from the shot's own directed REACTION), applies it AFTER the paired pose re-composes the stand-off mark (the recoil rides on top of the mark, never under it), and the evidence rides the state (`partnerReaction` + the stage's partner line: "the partner takes the hit: Demon Lord Wei staggers x1 (max 0.062m, recover f20)"). (4) THE STRIP SAYS WHICH FRAME IT IS (`identity.ts`): the vision layer kept miscounting its own filmstrip ("frame 5 of 3") - every strip frame now carries a BURNED-IN corner label ("frame 2/3 - 1.10s", drawtext with a bold system font, degrading honestly to unlabeled strips when no font exists) and both prompts (the identity scorer's and the inspection's) name the label and demand the model trust it over its own count. THE NIGHT (6/6 real Blender clips after two honest re-drains: the first S001 take overflowed to SIM when the submit hit the pool mid-restart, the retake hit the same race and the driver's redo scope caught it - the pool was warm on the third take): the evidence ON THE WIRE (anime:v120, face-v1 with 16 structures and 526 verts moved at 4mm max, wardrobe with 240 folds + HemTrim + CuffBands + ChestBandMesh, forge open on every build, the partner stagger on S006). THE EYE TEST: S003's closeup face now READS - the brow ridge sits under the brow line, the nose has form in 3/4 view, the eye sockets carry depth, the painted features and the sculpted form agree; S004's skirt folds read as cloth ridges; S005/S006's duel performs on both bodies - the clash in the burst ring, Wei's guard UP, and at the settle Wei CROUCHED low (the answer table's recoil pose AND the physics dip on one body); the strips carry their labels. THE DISTRIBUTION (median of 3): mean 38, median 38, p10 35 (119: 40/35/30 - p10 +5, median +3, mean -2 inside the vision-variance band the 116 finding named), refused 0, Wei's cell 30 -> 35, wardrobe cells reading 50 where the trims show - THE GATE REFUSED VERBATIM (BELOW the 70% floor; the style cells 10-40 stay the ceiling: "extremely simplified, lacking facial detail and intricate embroidery" vs the 2D sheets). THE STEER, IN ORDER: (1) the STYLE law - the distribution's honest ceiling is the painted read (toon-band ramps per material, line weight, the 2D-painted surface treatment; the craft geometry is now there for it to live on), (2) the face PAINT at establishing scale (the sculpt's sub-mm form is invisible at 20px - the drawn language must carry the structure: brow mass, nose shadow, the lash weight), (3) the anchor-sheet framing mismatch (S003's wardrobe cell read 10 because the closeup crops the sword and belt the sheet owns - the judge dings what the frame cannot show; a pose-matched sheet crop or a visible-aspects note ends the false ding), (4) the tassel and the partner's cloth riding the secondary rig (the stagger is published as _stagger; the partner's own cloth chains do not read it yet). PROOFS: blender-craft120-smoke 15 checks ALL GREEN (v120 pin, folds applied AND harmonically read on the mesh - FFT separates the 6th/9th fold harmonics from the ellipse's 2nd, hem trim, cuffs, embroidered decal real-UV'd and shrinkwrapping RobeBody, 16 face structures, sub-mm honest displacement, the profile reads proud in mm, bit-exact twice, spec-driven brow, sword forge, staff craft), blender-paired-smoke 16 checks ALL GREEN (the 119 nine plus: REACTION-only partner rig, honest skips, the stagger fires and points away from the striker, the spring returns the body, the stagger velocity published, NEWTON ACROSS RIGS), blender-anatomy/toon/anime/camera/addons smokes ALL GREEN (the anime + anatomy smokes pin v120), e2e-iter120-craft ALL GREEN (the REAL ffmpeg pass proves the labels drew - labeled bytes differ from the raw frames - plus the paired-law regression, the cue law, every wiring pin across the four laws), e2e-iter119 + e2e-iter118 + e2e-iter117 + smoke-choreographer ALL GREEN (the 119/118 pins legitimately advanced to 120 with the law bump), e2e-iter98 D2-D9 GREEN (D1's render half the named isolation seam), src tsc 0 errors, eslint clean on the touched files. 27. **THE STYLE LAW - the toon ramps + the line weight answer the framing, the face paint answers the establishing scale, and the anchor sheet answers in the shot's framing.** The 120 night's distribution named the ceiling twice ("the style cells stay the named ceiling") and the 121 laws aim at it on three fronts, every one a LAW OF THE FRAMING rather than a constant. (1) THE TOON RAMP ANSWERS THE FRAMING (`toon_pass.py` STYLE_RAMP_BY_SHOT): a drawn frame's bands are a graphic decision - as the figure shrinks toward establishing scale the lit band NARROWS (Toon Size 0.62 -> 0.46), the shadow DEEPENS (the flat floor scales to 0.80x) and the band edge HARDENS (Smooth 0.04 -> 0.02, a 3px band cannot afford a soft edge - it reads as mud, a hard edge reads as ink), while the tight framings keep the earned 113/115 close look exactly (the ramp refuses to touch them - `_cel_tree` takes the ramp, `style_ramp_for` answers per shot type, the evidence rides `look.styleRamp`). (2) THE LINE WEIGHT SOLVES FROM THE FRAMING: the hull offset was a constant 0.005 figure-local units - at the closeup's 0.5m that is ~12px of ink (a heavy brush) and at the establishing's 2.3m it is ~0.7px (SUB-PIXEL: the ink vanished and the wide read "3D low-poly" - exactly the cell that would not move); anime line weight is a call about the LINE ON SCREEN, so `ink_offset_for` solves the world offset that renders the target pixel width at the shot's own pixels-per-world (`offset = target_px / (res_x * lens / (dist * sensor))`, HULL_INK_PX 1.4/2.2 PREVIEW/FINAL), bounded 0.0012..0.024 world units - never a blob, never invisible; the worker builds the framing context from the framing table's own distance (multiplier x the figure's 0.9m law height), the shot's lens and the REAL output width, and the turnaround keeps the classic constant (a model sheet's cams are canon). (3) THE FACE PAINT ANSWERS THE ESTABLISHING SCALE (`FACE_PAINT_BY_SHOT` + `stage_face_paint_for_framing`): the 113 laws tuned the painted face for the distance that reads it and at the other end of the table the same paint VANISHES (at 2.3m the whole head is ~20px and the eye decals land under 1px - the face reads as a blank egg and the judge reads "face lacks detail"); the anime answer is a known craft move - the wide-shot face is not the closeup face scaled down, it is RE-DRAWN - so at WIDE and wider the six decal meshes (eyes, brows, mouth, nose) VERTEX-SCALE up (1.45/1.30/1.15/1.10 at establishing, stepped down through WIDE and LOW_ANGLE) and the decal emissions push to full strength, mesh-data scale only because sync_rig owns the eye decals' object scale for the blink, the shrinkwrap re-projecting the scaled plane onto the head; MEDIUM and tighter keep the 113 paint exactly (the staging refuses honestly, and the evidence rides `render.facePaint`). (4) THE ANCHOR SHEET ANSWERS IN THE SHOT'S FRAMING (`src/lib/identity.ts`): a canonical model sheet is a full-body standing turnaround and a shot is FRAMED - judging a head-and-shoulders clip against only the full-body sheet made the judge score the FRAMING, not the character (S002's wardrobe 0% - "the framing shows head+shoulders"); the turnaround's five views already render to disk next to the stitched sheet, so `framingViewFor` maps the shot's framing to the like-for-like view (CLOSEUP/ECU/MCU -> close, MEDIUM -> three, WIDE/ESTABLISHING/LOW_ANGLE/WS -> front), `prepareIdentityContext` attaches it as a second image right after its sheet when the file exists (only generated turnarounds carry views - an uploaded sheet rides alone), and the prompt states THE FRAMING CONTRACT: prefer the framing reference for close comparisons, judge each aspect ONLY on what the framing reveals, OMIT an aspect the framing cannot show rather than scoring it low and say "framing hides <aspect>" - the judge scores the character, never the framing. PROOFS: blender-toon-smoke ALL GREEN (the ramp ordering + the solved offset at both ends in bounds + the world-offset/distance law + the ramp riding a real cel tree + the six decals vertex-scaled x1.45 with the rig's blink scale untouched and a rig sync not undoing the mesh scale + the worker evidence naming the MEDIUM ramp, the solved ink and the untouched tight-framing paint), blender-anime/camera smokes ALL GREEN, e2e-iter121-style ALL GREEN (the framing view mapping, the prompt's image manifest + framing contract + honest fallbacks, the source-law assertions across the three fronts, and every generated turnaround carrying its three framing views on disk), e2e-iter120 + e2e-iter119 + e2e-iter118 + e2e-iter117 + e2e-iter109 ALL GREEN, src tsc 0 errors, eslint clean on the touched files. Tools: scripts/e2e-iter121-style.ts, scripts/provision-runtime.mjs.

28. **THE STANDING PRODUCTION ANSWERS THE RESET - the committed restore law, the designed turnaround answering the framing reference first, the one-import law that un-killed S006, and the re-score night confirming the distribution on a fresh production.** The director's proceed ordered the re-score night on the 121 build; the night first demanded the production EXIST - the sandbox reset had wiped the DB again, and the restore path was archaeology with a half-law (113 re-RAN the design crew, re-rolling the asset the distributions measured; 114 wired only Lin Yue, so Wei silently lost his 115-designed spec after every reset). THE COMMITTED RESTORE LAW (`scripts/night122-restore.ts`): the auto-seed brings the rows back, the four sheets anchor idempotently, Lin Yue's COMMITTED r2 and Wei's COMMITTED r2 wire BYTE-EXACT from git (`public/designs/cmuq1s4i.../r2/dna.json`, `cmuqieinq.../r2/dna.json` - no re-rolls anywhere, the confound dead by construction), the sheet DNA reads for the whole cast - the production is now REPRODUCIBLE end to end. THE DESIGNED TURNAROUND ANSWERS THE FRAMING REFERENCE FIRST (`src/lib/identity.ts` + `CastMember.designSheetUrl`): the 121 anchor-sheet law was dead code on the standing production - `framingViewUrl` required `modelSheetUrl` to end in `turn_sheet.png` but nothing in src ever points it there (the crew lands `designSheetUrl`), so the like-for-like framing reference never attached where the night scores; the fix resolves the reference design-FIRST (the designed build IS what the pipeline constructs - its own per-view renders are the like-for-like truth), the anchored image-gen sheet stays the canonical art, and the e2e grew section 8 asserting the order and both committed turnarounds carrying their three framing views on disk. ONE IMPORT, BOTH BLOCKS (`bridges/blender/animeos_bridge.py`): S006 died with "cannot access local variable 'choreography_pass'" - the conditional imports made the name a function-local bound on some paths only, and a resident worker that already rendered an inline-choreo shot skips the paired block's import (the sys.modules hit) and reads the unbound local; the import binds unconditionally above both blocks and the paired smoke stays ALL GREEN. THE NIGHT (median of 3, hull ink, POOL=1): 6/6 real Blender clips (S006's redo rendered clean through the fixed bridge - the paired path builds AND renders), scored 6 refused 0, THE DISTRIBUTION mean 47% / median 35% / p10 35% vs the 70% floor - verdict BELOW (4/6 under), the 120 territory confirmed on a fresh production; S006 the night's best shot (Lin Yue 70% AND Wei 70% - the paired duel READS, hair 80 / wardrobe 80 / palette 90); the gate refused the publish verbatim with every under cell named (calm/emission/med/shadow, the worst shot named per cell). 54. **ITERATION 142 - THE ARC: the release gate's number is the multi-night median, not a single night's sweep.** The 141 record named the instrument question and the 142 probe answered it before any pen moved (probe-142-arc.ts on the two recorded boards - 140's night 35/35/35/35/35/50 and 141's night 35/50/85/65/45/80, identical craft under the standing ANIME 125 / TOON 133 / PRESENCE 108): the standing single-night sweep swings 23 points of mean between two drains of the same pixels (not reproducible); the per-shot median ACROSS nights lands mean 49 / median 46 - ONE number, converging as nights accumulate, and still BELOW the 70 floor (the instrument does not flatter the craft); a 10k-sample bootstrap proves more within-night samples cannot see across the drain-to-drain band. THE STRUCTURAL FIND: the sweep's own table cannot remember - IdentityScore carries ONE row per shot+source (every night overwrites the last), so the release gate has only ever read one night, however many nights the studio burned. THE MECHANISM: a new IdentityArcReading ledger (unique per shot+source+night, cohort-indexed), appended by the night rescore (night-tagged, cohort-tagged from the bridges' own law versions - a125/t133/p108), NEVER touched by the reset's identityScore wipe; the release gate rolls the PER-ENTRY MEDIAN across the latest cohort's nights; a law bump opens a new cohort automatically (old readings never pollute new laws); a one-night arc answers PROVISIONAL in its own refusal words; the pre-arc fallback answers byte-exact (the cascade's standing refusal shapes cannot change).
